14:T638,### Problem

On develop `229502c357`, `plugins/plugin-assistant/src/features/messaging/triage/actions/listInbox.ts` serves the cached TriageService store whenever it is non-empty and only triggers a live `triage()` pull when the cache is completely empty. `MessageRefStore` keeps every ref a sweep ever handed it and never prunes read rows, so once the cached view contains only read messages, the action permanently answers "No unread messages across connected platforms." for the rest of the process lifetime while the authoritative connectors hold new unread mail.

### Reproduction and expected behavior

1. A `triage` sweep stores refs that are all `isRead` (or every ref in the store becomes read).
2. New unread mail arrives on the connector.
3. MESSAGE `list_inbox` ("show me unread across all platforms") → the cached path runs `rankScored`, filters unread to zero, and reports "No unread messages across connected platforms." without consulting any adapter.

Expected: a cached view with zero unread must fall through to the same live pull an empty cache gets, so the empty answer is only reported after the authoritative sources agree. An empty result after that live pull stays truthful.

### Acceptance

- An all-read cache triggers exactly one live pull; the returned unread view comes from the live result.
- When the live pull also yields no unread, the explicit empty-inbox result is reported (now live-verified, and the adapter pull is observable).
- Existing cached-unread and empty-cache behaviors are unchanged.
- Tests cover both cases against the real action handler.15:T489,Hi @lalalune @lumix5,

First off, genuine respect for this project — an open-source agentic OS with this
breadth (multi-platform desktop app, plugin runtime, agent API) is rare, and the
engineering quality shows. Case in point: my security report got a fix PR within
about an hour, and both issues were verified with regression tests and closed
within two days. That turnaround is among the best I've seen in open source.

I'm the reporter of #33034 and #33036. Since both are already fixed on develop
(#33040, #33142, #33169), I'm opening this only as a follow-up to a request I
left on both threads on Oct 4, which I suspect went unnoticed — quite
understandable, since both were closed by then.

## The request

Could you help get CVE IDs assigned for these two vulnerabilities?

For context on why it matters: users running `@elizaos/app` ≤ 2.0.3-beta.7 are
exposed to silent theft of the OWNER-level token / plaintext stored provider
keys by any website they visit. A published GHSA + CVE is how most users and
scanners learn that a desktop update is security-critical, and it also records
how quickly you fixed it.

Thanks again for the fast fixes!
16:T9d6,**Describe the bug**

#34093 / #34094 route memory-content replacement, atomic-publication heads, and `updateLogs` through the jsonb sanitizer. The rest of the entity-graph writes in `plugins/plugin-sql/src/base.ts` still bind caller objects through drizzle's default `JSON.stringify` (or a hand-built `JSON.stringify(...)::jsonb`), with no check for text PostgreSQL jsonb cannot store. A NUL character or a lone UTF-16 surrogate (typically a truncated emoji) in entity, component, room, world, task, relationship, cache or agent data fails in the driver:

- Some writes surface the raw `22P02` / `22P05` `DrizzleQueryError`.
- Others wrap it in a generic `DB_*_FAILED`.

The memory path returns the typed `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_NUL` / `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_SURROGATE` instead. In both cases the drizzle `Failed query` message embeds the bound parameters, so the caller's data goes into any log that records the error.

**To Reproduce**

On `origin/develop` `65621da3a4` (with #34094 applied), real PGlite via `createIsolatedTestDatabase`. Each write gets `{ note: "x \ud83d" }` in its jsonb field:

| Write | Result |
|---|---|
| `updateEntity` metadata | raw `22P02`, `Failed query: update "entities" …` |
| `createComponent` data | raw `22P02`, `Failed query: insert into "components" …` |
| `updateRoom` metadata | raw `22P02`, `Failed query: update "rooms" …` |
| `updateWorld` metadata | raw `22P02`, `Failed query: update "worlds" …` |
| `createTask` metadata | raw `22P02`, `Failed query: insert into "tasks" …` |
| `createEntities` metadata | `DB_INSERT_FAILED` wrapping the raw error |
| `createRelationship` metadata | `DB_INSERT_FAILED` wrapping the raw error |
| `setCache` value | `DB_UPSERT_FAILED` wrapping the raw error |
| `updateAgent` settings | `DB_UPDATE_FAILED` wrapping the raw error |

Other sites with the same binding:
- `createAgent`, `updateComponent`, `createRooms`, `updateRelationship`, `createWorld`, `updateTask`;
- `compareAndSwapWorldMetadata`, `patchComponents`, `updatePendingTask`, `patchTaskMetadata`;
- the cache compare-and-swap.

**Expected behavior**

These writes reject unsupported text before the bind with the same typed `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_NUL` / `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_SURROGATE` errors, carrying no content.

Routing them through `serializeJsonb` would also apply its 1 MiB / 10,000-node / depth-64 structural caps and cycle rewriting. That would add new hard limits to component, task and agent data that have none today, so the check should be text-only.
17:T24f9,**Describe the bug**

Three jsonb writes in `plugins/plugin-sql/src/base.ts` bind caller text without the shared sanitizer in `plugins/plugin-sql/src/sanitize-json.ts`. A NUL character or a lone UTF-16 surrogate (half of a pair, typically a truncated emoji) reaches PostgreSQL and fails with the driver's raw `22P05` / `22P02` error. The matching create paths return a typed error instead: `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_NUL` / `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_SURROGATE` for memory rows, and for log bodies the NUL is stripped and the surrogate replaced with U+FFFD. #33124 added the lone-surrogate half of that policy to the sanitizer, but these three writes never call it.

- `base.ts:2223`, `publishMessageContentSegments` in replace mode: `.set({ content: params.replacementContent })` passes the content object to drizzle, which binds `JSON.stringify(value)`. Create mode of the same method writes the parent through `insertMemoryInTransaction` (`base.ts:2122`), which applies the strict serializer to content and metadata (`base.ts:4672-4680`). The public `runtime.replaceMessageMemoryContent` (`packages/core/src/runtime.ts:5264`, `packages/core/src/runtime/data-mutations.ts:413-470`) reaches this branch. plugin-assistant calls it through `replaceStoredMessageContent` after attachment enrichment, after an incoming hook rewrites the text, and after translation (`plugins/plugin-assistant/src/services/message/processor.ts:526`, `:581`, `:1110`). Text under the 64 KiB inline limit stays in the parent row, so it reaches this write. Longer text goes to segment rows, which are already sanitized.
- `base.ts:7978-7979`, `compareAndSwapMemoryPublication`: the head's content and metadata are bound as `JSON.stringify(...)` with a `::jsonb` cast. The dependencies of the same publication go through `insertMemoryInTransaction`.
- `base.ts:7890`, `updateLogs`: `setValues.body = updates.body` binds the new body unsanitized. `log()` passes the body through `sanitizeJsonObject` first (`base.ts:4052`).

The raw error is also a worse failure to surface: drizzle's `Failed query` message embeds the bound parameters, so the whole content goes into any log that records the error. The typed errors carry no content.

**To Reproduce**

On `origin/develop` `65621da3a4`, with Bun 1.3.14, Node 24.15.0 and `bun install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts`. The probe uses a real PGlite adapter and the real `AgentRuntime` from `createIsolatedTestDatabase` (`plugins/plugin-sql/src/__tests__/test-helpers.ts`), and creates a room and an entity first:

```ts
await adapter.createMemory({ id, agentId, entityId, roomId, content: { text: "original" }, metadata: { type: "message" } }, "messages");
await runtime.replaceMessageMemoryContent(id, { text: "x \ud83d" });
await runtime.replaceMessageMemoryContent(id, { text: "x \u0000" });
await adapter.compareAndSwapMemoryPublication({ head: { memory: { id: headId, agentId, entityId, roomId, content: { text: "x \ud83d" }, metadata: { revision: "r1" } }, tableName: "probe_heads" }, dependencies: [], expectedRevision: null });
await adapter.compareAndSwapMemoryPublication({ /* same head */ content: { text: "ok" }, metadata: { revision: "r1", note: "a\u0000b" } });
await adapter.log({ body: { reply: "first" }, entityId, roomId, type: "probe" });
await adapter.updateLogs([{ id: logId, updates: { body: { reply: "done \ud83d", nested: { text: "a\u0000b" } } } }]);
```

Probe output, verbatim (the error message is cut at 160 characters by the probe):

```
replaceMessageMemoryContent text lone surrogate: THROWS Error | code=undefined | message="Failed query: update \"memories\" set \"content\" = $1 where \"memories\".\"id\" = $2 returning \"id\", \"type\", \"created_at\", \"content\", \"entity_id\", \"agent_id\", \"room_id" | cause.name=error | cause.code=22P02 | cause.message="invalid input syntax for type json"
replaceMessageMemoryContent text lone surrogate: stored content after = {"text":"original"}
replaceMessageMemoryContent text NUL: THROWS Error | code=undefined | message="Failed query: update \"memories\" set \"content\" = $1 where \"memories\".\"id\" = $2 returning \"id\", \"type\", \"created_at\", \"content\", \"entity_id\", \"agent_id\", \"room_id" | cause.name=error | cause.code=22P05 | cause.message="unsupported Unicode escape sequence"
replaceMessageMemoryContent text NUL: stored content after = {"text":"original"}
compareAndSwapMemoryPublication content lone surrogate: THROWS Error | code=undefined | message="Failed query: insert into \"memories\" (\"id\", \"type\", \"created_at\", \"content\", \"entity_id\", \"agent_id\", \"room_id\", \"world_id\", \"unique\", \"metadata\") values ($1, $" | cause.name=error | cause.code=22P02 | cause.message="invalid input syntax for type json"
compareAndSwapMemoryPublication content lone surrogate: stored rows after = 0
compareAndSwapMemoryPublication metadata NUL: THROWS Error | code=undefined | message="Failed query: insert into \"memories\" (\"id\", \"type\", \"created_at\", \"content\", \"entity_id\", \"agent_id\", \"room_id\", \"world_id\", \"unique\", \"metadata\") values ($1, $" | cause.name=error | cause.code=22P05 | cause.message="unsupported Unicode escape sequence"
compareAndSwapMemoryPublication metadata NUL: stored rows after = 0
updateLogs lone surrogate + NUL: THROWS Error | code=undefined | message="Failed query: update \"logs\" set \"body\" = $1 where (\"logs\".\"id\" = $2 and \"logs\".\"room_id\" in (select \"id\" from \"rooms\" where \"rooms\".\"agent_id\" = $3))\nparams: {\"" | cause.name=error | cause.code=22P02 | cause.message="invalid input syntax for type json"
updateLogs: stored body after = {"reply":"first"}
```

The complete error for the first call, as Vitest prints it (`runtime.replaceMessageMemoryContent(id, { text: "x \ud83d" })`, real PGlite). The message ends with the bound parameters, which hold the content:

```
DrizzleQueryError {
  "message": "Failed query: update \"memories\" set \"content\" = $1 where \"memories\".\"id\" = $2 returning \"id\", \"type\", \"created_at\", \"content\", \"entity_id\", \"agent_id\", \"room_id\", \"world_id\", \"unique\", \"metadata\"
params: {\"text\":\"x \\ud83d\"},31809931-c3e8-4f75-8d43-491cf88ee69d",
  "cause": error {
    "message": "invalid input syntax for type json",
    "code": "22P02",
    "detail": "Unicode low surrogate must follow a high surrogate.",
```

**Expected behavior**

Each write applies the policy its create path already applies:

- The replacement parent and the publication head reject with `ElizaError` `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_SURROGATE` or `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_NUL`, as `createMemory` does. Nothing is written: the parent is unchanged, and no segment or dependency row is staged.
- `updateLogs` stores the body with NUL removed and lone surrogates replaced by U+FFFD, as `log()` does.
- Well-formed surrogate pairs such as emoji are stored unchanged.

**Screenshots / recording of the wrong behavior (required for anything visible)**

N/A - storage-adapter behaviour with no rendered surface. The transcripts above are the proof.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- [x] MP4 recording or JPG screenshots of the broken behavior: N/A - no visible surface; see the transcripts.
- [x] Backend logs and frontend console/network logs when relevant: the adapter's thrown errors and the stored rows are above. N/A for frontend: no frontend code is involved.
- [x] Real-LLM trajectory when the bug involves agent/action/prompt/model behavior: N/A - no model, prompt or action is involved. The probe calls the runtime API directly.
- [x] Domain artifacts when relevant: the stored message content, publication row count and log body after each call are in the transcript.

**Additional context**

- Follow-up to #33124 (#33125), which taught the shared sanitizer to handle lone surrogates.
- The two in-repo `compareAndSwapMemoryPublication` callers, `plugins/plugin-google-workspace/src/gmail-content-cache.ts:433` and `plugins/plugin-assistant/src/features/advanced-memory/session-summary-content-manifest.ts:505`, put `JSON.stringify(...)` output in `content.text`, which already escapes NUL and lone surrogates. They do not hit the raw error today. The gap is in the adapter method itself, and in the head's metadata values.
- No in-repo caller passes a body to `updateLogs`. Plugins reach it through `runtime.updateLogs` (`packages/core/src/runtime.ts:4674`).
- Out of scope here: other jsonb writes. #33124 noted that cache, component, entity and room metadata writes do not use this sanitizer either.

18:T772,`chunkMarkdownText` (`packages/core/src/markdown/chunk.ts`) can emit an empty code block, then reopen the fence in the next chunk, when a fenced block's closing line is longer than `indent + marker`. That covers trailing spaces or tabs (`"``` "`), a longer closing marker (```` ```` ```` closing ```` ``` ````), an indented closer (`"  ```"`), and `"~~~ "`. Every outbound text that goes through the markdown chunker is affected.

## Reproduction (current `develop` `65621da3a42`, Bun 1.4.2)

```ts
import { chunkMarkdownText } from "@elizaos/core";
const body = Array.from({ length: 10 }, (_, i) => `line ${i} value${"x".repeat(i % 4)}`);
const text = `Intro.\n\n\`\`\`ts\n${body.join("\n")}\n\`\`\` \n\nOutro paragraph.`;
chunkMarkdownText(text, 16);
// [..., "```ts\nline 9\n```", "```ts\n``` \n", "Outro paragraph."]
```

Across limits 16..110 with the same 10-line block, empty code-block chunks per closing line:

| Closing line | `"```"` | `"``` "` | `"```  "` | `"```\t"` | ```` "````" ```` | `"~~~ "` | `"  ```"` | `"  ``` "` | ```` "``````   " ```` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Empty blocks | 0 | 4 | 8 | 4 | 4 | 4 | 8 | 11 | 23 |

## Cause

When a split lands inside a fence, the chunker reserves room only for the synthetic closer `${indent}${marker}`. If the original closing line is longer, there are limits where the chunk fits the synthetic closer but not the real one. The cut then lands at the start of the real closing line, and the next chunk reopens the fence (`openLine + "\n"`) just to close it again.

Once #34057 (CRLF fence recognition) merges, CRLF input hits the same path, because the `\r` makes every CRLF closing line one character longer.

## Expected

When the cut leaves no fence content, close the fence in the current chunk and drop the original closing line. No chunk should be an empty code block, and code content and the chunk limit stay unchanged.
19:Tcb0,**Describe the bug**

In `plugins/plugin-wallet/src/sdk/x402/client.ts`, a payment is checked with `this.budget.checkBudget(service, amount)` (sync), then `await this.executePayment(selected)` (an on-chain transfer), and only afterwards recorded with `this.budget.recordPayment(log)`. `X402BudgetTracker` keeps `dailySpend` in memory and only updates it in `recordPayment`. So concurrent x402 requests to the same service can all pass the per-service `dailyLimit` check before any of them is recorded, and the agent spends past the configured service cap.

Scope, to be precise: the AgentWallet contract's on-chain spend policy (`setSpendPolicy` / `remainingBudget`) still bounds global spend when it's configured. What can be exceeded is the client-side per-service caps (`serviceBudgets[].dailyLimit`) and `globalDailyLimit` when no on-chain policy is set.

**To Reproduce**

A minimal script against the real `budget.ts` (at `f7a6b0fea5`) that mirrors the client's check → await → record order:

```ts
import { X402BudgetTracker } from "./budget.ts";
const tracker = new X402BudgetTracker({
  serviceBudgets: [{ service: "api.example.com", maxPerRequest: 1_000_000n, dailyLimit: 5_000_000n }], // $5/day
});
const executePayment = () => new Promise<void>((r) => setTimeout(r, 50)); // stands in for the transfer
async function pay(i: number) {
  if (!tracker.checkBudget("api.example.com", 1_000_000n).allowed) return "blocked";
  await executePayment();
  tracker.recordPayment({ timestamp: Date.now(), service: "api.example.com", url: `https://api.example.com/${i}`,
    amount: 1_000_000n, token: "0x00…00", recipient: "0x00…01", success: true } as any);
  return "paid";
}
const outcomes = await Promise.all(Array.from({ length: 10 }, (_, i) => pay(i)));
```

Output (`bun run repro.ts`):

```
10 concurrent $1 payments against a $5/day service cap -> paid=10, blocked=0, recorded spend=$10
next check after they settle: allowed=false (Would exceed daily limit 5000000 for api.example.com)
```

**Expected behavior**

At most 5 of the 10 payments proceed, and the rest are blocked before `executePayment` runs.

**Screenshots / recording of the wrong behavior (required for anything visible)**

N/A - not a visible bug; the reproduction output above is the evidence.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- [ ] MP4 recording or JPG screenshots: N/A - logic race, no UI.
- [x] Logs: script output above.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectory: N/A - independent of the model.
- [x] Domain artifacts: recorded spend $10 against a $5 service cap.

**Additional context**

A small fix that keeps the existing API: have `checkBudget` *reserve* the amount synchronously when it allows a payment (add it to a pending total that the daily checks include), then turn the reservation into spend in `recordPayment`, or release it if `executePayment` throws or fails. Because there's no `await` between the check and the hold, concurrent requests can't both pass against the same remaining budget. Totals would still be in memory, so restarts reset them; that seems acceptable as long as the on-chain policy is the hard limit.

I'm happy to send that as a PR with a test based on the reproduction above. (I maintain Inferrail, where I fixed the same pattern.)

1c:T4a8,## Problem

`describeRecurrence` always formats a rule's `UNTIL` date with `timeZone: "UTC"`.

For Google Calendar–style **date-time** UNTIL values, the instant is the end of the **event's local day** converted to UTC. Google's documented split example for a 10am `America/Los_Angeles` series uses `UNTIL=20110617T065959Z`, which is 23:59:59 PDT on **June 16** — the series ends on the 16th locally. Rendering that instant in UTC produces **June 17**, so action replies that say the event "repeats … until …" are one calendar day late (same for New York end-of-day `T045959Z` / `T035959Z` forms).

Date-only UNTIL (`YYYYMMDD`) is already a named calendar date (parsed as 23:59:59Z) and should stay on that UTC date label.

## Repro

```ts
describeRecurrence(
  ["RRULE:FREQ=WEEKLY;UNTIL=20110617T065959Z"],
  "America/Los_Angeles",
);
// develop: "weekly until Jun 17, 2011"
// expected: "weekly until Jun 16, 2011"
```

## Expected

Date-time UNTIL labels use the event IANA timezone; date-only UNTIL stays on the stated calendar date.

## Area

`plugins/plugin-calendar/src/internal/recurrence.ts` — `describeRecurrence` / `formatUntilLabel`, call sites in `calendar-handler.ts`.
1d:T596,### Bug

`VideoService.getTranscript` (`plugins/plugin-video/src/services/video.ts`) takes the first `subtitles.en` variant that has a `url` and always parses it with `parseSRT`.

yt-dlp's YouTube extractor lists each manual subtitle language as variants in a fixed order: `json3, srv1, srv2, srv3, ttml, srt, vtt` (`_SUBTITLE_FORMATS` in `yt_dlp/extractor/youtube`). That makes the first usable variant `json3`, so for any YouTube video with manual English subtitles, `parseSRT` gets a JSON3 document. It drops the first two lines and joins the rest, and the transcript ends up as raw JSON:

```
"events": [ { "tStartMs": 1000, "dDurationMs": 3000, "segs": [ { "utf8": "hello there" } ] } ] }
```

`parseSRT` also breaks on WebVTT and on loosely formatted SRT:
- It always drops the first two lines of each block, so a WebVTT cue without an identifier loses its first text line (single-line cues vanish).
- It splits only on `\n\n`, so a whitespace-only separator line or bare-CR line endings merge cues, and the next cue's number and `-->` timing leak into the transcript (`Hello world! 2 00:00:04,500 --> 00:00:07,000 This is a test.`).

### Expected

Manual subtitles produce the cue text only (`hello there`).

### Repro

Mock yt-dlp info with `subtitles.en` = the YouTube variant list above, and return JSON3 for the `fmt=json3` URL and SRT for `fmt=srt`. `processVideo(...).text` comes back as JSON text, not the cue text.
1e:T483,### What happens
`parsePdfSpecDate` (`plugins/plugin-pdf/services/pdf-date.ts`) clamps each `D:` field independently (month 1–12, day 1–31, …). Calendar-impossible combinations that still pass those ranges — February 30, April 31, February 29 outside a leap year — are handed to `Date.setUTCFullYear` / `setFullYear`, which overflow into the next month. `PdfService.getDocumentInfo` then surfaces that invented instant as `CreationDate` / `ModDate`.

So a corrupt or hand-edited PDF metadata date becomes a plausible-looking wrong date instead of being dropped (the contract already stated for unparseable `D:` strings).

### Repro
```ts
parsePdfSpecDate("D:20240230120000Z")
// develop: 2024-03-01T12:00:00.000Z
// expected: undefined

parsePdfSpecDate("D:20240431120000Z")
// develop: 2024-05-01T12:00:00.000Z

parsePdfSpecDate("D:20230229120000Z")
// develop: 2023-03-01T12:00:00.000Z
```

### Expected
If the declared wall-clock is not a real calendar day, return `undefined` so metadata omits the date. Keep the existing out-of-range clamp defaults (`D:20241340Z` → January 1). Real leap days (`D:20240229…`, year 0 Feb 29) stay valid.
1f:T5b9,`IDatabaseAdapter.searchEntitiesByName` documents a case-insensitive substring search whose results are "ordered by relevance (exact matches first)", capped at `limit`. The SQL adapter (`plugins/plugin-sql/src/base.ts`, `searchEntitiesByName`) doesn't do that:

1. **No ORDER BY in either branch.** Both the empty-query Drizzle query and the substring `db.execute` query apply `LIMIT` with no `ORDER BY`. The page is whatever physical order the heap returns, so "the first N entities" depends on write history (imports, restores, row rewrites), not on the data.
2. **No exact-match priority.** A query that exactly equals an entity's name can be cut off by `limit` in favour of older partial matches.
3. **LIKE metacharacters are not escaped.** The query goes straight into `LIKE '%' || query || '%'`, so a query of `%` or `_` matches every entity for the agent, and `a_b` matches `axb`. Memory text search in the same file already escapes with `escapeIlikeLiteral(...) … ESCAPE '\'`.

**Repro (PGlite, develop a853470):** insert three entities whose physical order differs from `created_at`, then call `searchEntitiesByName({ query: "", limit: 2 })` and `({ query: "probe", limit: 2 })`. You get the physically first rows, not the oldest two. Call `({ query: "%" })` and you get every entity.

**Expected:** a stable total order (`created_at, id`, matching `queryEntities` after #33119), exact matches first for non-empty queries, and `%`/`_` matched literally.

# Change

The fix uses the existing capability, permission, consent, proposal and receipt gates. It adds guidance for current effects, not a new native effect. - Earlier isolated qualification of this source contract passed 140 core/host/assistant tests, core standard typecheck, aligned host/assistant typechecks, and 20-file lint. Compact briefing main-source checks passed 77 PA tests and 39 actual source integration tests. Full pinned repository verification passed on combined source `a4e988598533f311f4e8b190ee0681534d7ceabe`.
- The final two corrected files match qualified main exactly. Two other files intentionally retain earlier context-lane differences in tool-message compaction placement and direct-route coverage wiring; unrelated context changes were not copied into this PR. Current checked `develop` is `65621da3a424f8fcdce760db5c9c414117082e18`, already included.
- Live original-brief acceptance on the final combined host used three foreground calls, 50,396 input tokens (29,696 cached), 988 output tokens, and 3.528 seconds. Planner and evaluator carried the authenticated source notice without the full addressable-control block or an extra restoration call. The notice hash matched the retained 5,468-byte full snapshot. - Previous operation-schema/definition-read acceptance used three calls, 53,468 input tokens (2,048 cached), 1,176 output tokens, and 4.843 seconds; the earlier unresolved-operation run used four calls, 72,790 input tokens, and 7.208 seconds. The intermediate compact-JSON run used 52,680 input tokens and 5.443 seconds while the full active-view block still persisted. These are observed runs, not an isolated causal benchmark: cache warmth, date/current facts, and expired reminders differed. The removed resolver edge and final source projection are directly evidenced; the entire latency delta is not attributed solely to schema changes.

The aggregate 20,000-input-token target remains unmet. No fixed call-count or latency guarantee is made. Live acceptance includes complementary context work in #34032; this branch's focused checks do not establish independent universal runtime/device readiness. Private captures remain local.

Keep this PR a draft. CI, review, and release approval remain separate; no merge, promotion, or deployment is requested.
27:Tac3,# Change

Fixes concrete defects found during the open PR and issue review. Calendar summaries keep all-day civil dates. Blocker status does not become stale after a late read. SOL transfers reject amounts that cannot be represented exactly before signer access. Branch caches remain separate for each credential. Empty workflow input stays empty. Entity search treats percent and underscore literally and returns exact matches first. PDF dates reject impossible days while keeping local daylight-saving behavior. Video transcripts use supported subtitle formats and retain the existing fallback sources.

Also includes targeted saved-fact history guidance, JSON extraction requests, normalized keyword matching, a retained-error policy comment, and the reviewed contribution guidance.

# Relates to

Integrates original ancestry from #34162, #34163, #34164, #34171, #34172, #34173, #34174, #34175, #34177, #34179, #34181, and #34183. Resolves the source defects in #34168, #34176, #34178, and #34182 after merge.

Clock #34167 stays in draft because physical-phone acceptance is incomplete. Its source and UI test repair remain in #34180. Production promotion #33708 also stays separate.

# Why this implementation

Uses the current parsers, caches, formatting helpers, scheduler inputs, and transport boundaries. Review repairs cover observed races and incorrect output. They do not add a new service or change ownership rules.

# Testing

Uses Bun 1.4.2 and Node 24.15.0. The combined local integration passed repository verification (298 tasks), focused owner tests, and the app browser checks. This branch contains the same reviewed fix source, with Clock removed. Fresh verification and hosted checks for this exact branch are running.

Focused results include Calendar 53, wallet 19, blocker 60, PDF 66, video 63 (4 existing skips), extraction 6, workflow 5, credential cache 4, and SQL search 4 passing tests. Detailed full-package results will be added before merge.

## Known gaps / failures

The full combined-root test attempt found timeouts in unchanged restore and retention tests on macOS. No timeout was increased and no unproven restore optimization is included. These failures remain visible in the final report; this is not a claim that the whole repository test suite is green. Device, funded-provider, and production deployment acceptance are not established by these source checks.

## How to test

Run the owning package tests, typecheck, and lint with the pinned runtime, then `bun run verify`. Inspect the changed regression cases for all-day dates, concurrent cache mutation, fractional SOL amounts, literal entity search, DST date parsing, and subtitle fallback. No UI layout or native Clock feature is included in this branch.

Stale server declarations cannot restore the retired Home card under either historical plugin ID.

Once prompt automations now use the existing native trigger scheduler with an exact saved time and timezone. Creating a prompt binds delivery to the canonical owner conversation through the authenticated host; request-supplied room IDs are ignored. Runtime identity is checked before and after asynchronous room resolution. Missing destination rooms fail before saving or dispatching; workflows keep their autonomy-room path. Visible errors contain the message rather than stack fragments, with full diagnostics retained through `reportError`. Existing saved triggers are not migrated.

On conversation reload, the backend transcript GET now omits synthetic `trigger-prompt` inputs instead of displaying scheduler instructions as owner-authored user bubbles. The assistant result and ordinary user messages remain visible, including ordinary text identical to a scheduled instruction. Canonical stored rows and complete model-facing history are preserved. This is a GET-only display correction; it adds no model call and requires no Android or renderer rebuild.

The PR also preserves recovery-owned draft identity, binds reminder editors and requests to the active account, retains Android foreground presentation where native reminder push does not own it, and shares the existing runtime-mode subscription. Automations opens in Vite development: exact CommonJS selector-shim imports are prebundled through the existing optimizer instead of being served raw without their default export.

Updates `multidict` from 6.7.1 to 6.9.1
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/aio-libs/multidict/releases">multidict's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>6.9.1</h2>
<h2>Bug fixes</h2>
<ul>
<li>
<p>Fixed the C extension reading freed memory on free-threaded builds when a
list handed to :py:meth:<code>~multidict.MultiDict.update</code>,
:py:meth:<code>~multidict.MultiDict.extend</code>, :py:meth:<code>~multidict.MultiDict.merge</code>
or the :py:class:<code>~multidict.MultiDict</code> and :py:class:<code>~multidict.CIMultiDict</code>
constructors, a <code>[key, value]</code> item inside any iterable handed to them, or a
list tested with <code>in</code> against :py:meth:<code>~multidict.MultiDict.items</code>, is
changed by another thread; a call that catches the list shrinking under it
now raises :py:exc:<code>RuntimeError</code> -- by :user:<code>rodrigobnogueira</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
<a href="https://redirect.github.com/aio-libs/multidict/issues/1437">#1437</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed a data race on the free-threaded build where a retired hash table's
reader count used relaxed atomics, letting a lock-free <code>get()</code>/<code>getone()</code>/
<code>__getitem__()</code> read race a concurrent free of that table. The reader-exit
decrement and the drain's free check now use release/acquire ordering
instead -- by :user:<code>asvetlov</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
<a href="https://redirect.github.com/aio-libs/multidict/issues/1481">#1481</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed a free-threaded build bug where two threads calling <code>update()</code>,
<code>merge()</code>, or <code>__setitem__()</code> on the same key at the same time could lose
the key entirely instead of just racing on which value wins. A decref of the
replaced value could transiently suspend the writer's critical section,
letting a second writer for the same key observe the first writer's
in-progress entry as absent and, once both settled, mistake it for a stale
duplicate and delete it. Every such decref is now deferred until the writer
has released its critical section, so the window can no longer open.
<code>setdefault()</code> had an unrelated instance of the same blind spot (it could
insert a duplicate rather than recognizing an in-flight key), fixed alongside
it -- by :user:<code>asvetlov</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
<a href="https://redirect.github.com/aio-libs/multidict/issues/1483">#1483</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed a free-threaded build bug where <code>getall()</code> and the <code>items()</code>/
<code>keys()</code>/<code>values()</code> equality path could raise <code>KeyError</code> or report a
present, never-deleted key as missing. A concurrent <code>update()</code>/<code>extend()</code>/
<code>__setitem__()</code> call can have its critical section transiently suspended
(a decref triggering a blocking allocator call) while an entry is marked as
part of its own bookkeeping; a reader landing in that window used to treat
the mark as &quot;not found&quot; instead of &quot;still there, in flight&quot; -- by
:user:<code>asvetlov</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em></p>
</li>
</ul>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
</blockquote>
<p>... (truncated)</p>
</details>

<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/releases">werkzeug's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>3.1.9</h2>
<p>This is the Werkzeug 3.1.9 security fix release, which fixes security issues and bugs but does not otherwise change behavior and should not result in breaking changes compared to the latest feature release.</p>
<p>PyPI: <a href="https://pypi.org/project/Werkzeug/3.1.9/">https://pypi.org/project/Werkzeug/3.1.9/</a>
Changes: <a href="https://werkzeug.palletsprojects.com/page/changes/#version-3-1-9">https://werkzeug.palletsprojects.com/page/changes/#version-3-1-9</a>
Milestone: <a href="https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/milestone/46?closed=1">https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/milestone/46?closed=1</a></p>
<ul>
<li><code>safe_join</code> on Windows does not allow special devices names with empty ADS markers on NTFS. <a href="https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/security/advisories/GHSA-g6x2-hccm-hh4m">https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/security/advisories/GHSA-g6x2-hccm-hh4m</a></li>
<li><code>ProfilerMiddleware</code> uses <code>profiling.tracing</code> on Python 3.15. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3207">#3207</a></li>
<li><code>uri_to_iri</code> and <code>iri_to_uri</code> preserve empty username, password, and port 0. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3189">#3189</a></li>
<li>Improve performance of <code>parse_options_header</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3231">#3231</a></li>
<li>Improve performance of <code>parse_etags</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3231">#3231</a></li>
<li>Improve performance of <code>parse_cookie</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3231">#3231</a></li>
<li><code>get_host</code> also checks that the port is in the valid range. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3236">#3236</a></li>
<li>The <code>int</code> URL converter returns a 404 instead of 500 error when the value is longer than <code>sys.get_int_max_str_digits()</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3237">#3237</a></li>
<li>Improve debugger PIN generation from cgroup data inside Podman. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3245">#3245</a></li>
<li><code>Authorization</code> parsing <code>basic</code> auth disallows non-base64 characters.  <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3248">#3248</a></li>
<li><code>application/x-www-form-urlencoded</code> form data is no longer limited to <code>max_form_memory_size</code>, only <code>max_content_length</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3251">#3251</a></li>
<li><code>LimitedStream.readinto</code> does not resize the buffer when it reads less than the remaining size. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3253">#3253</a></li>
<li>Rules with 10 or more converters in a single part assign matched values correctly. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3254">#3254</a></li>
<li>The invalid <code>Range</code> suffix length <code>-0</code> is no longer accepted. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3255">#3255</a></li>
</ul>
</blockquote>
</details>

Updates `multidict` from 6.7.1 to 6.9.1
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/aio-libs/multidict/releases">multidict's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>6.9.1</h2>
<h2>Bug fixes</h2>
<ul>
<li>
<p>Fixed the C extension reading freed memory on free-threaded builds when a
list handed to :py:meth:<code>~multidict.MultiDict.update</code>,
:py:meth:<code>~multidict.MultiDict.extend</code>, :py:meth:<code>~multidict.MultiDict.merge</code>
or the :py:class:<code>~multidict.MultiDict</code> and :py:class:<code>~multidict.CIMultiDict</code>
constructors, a <code>[key, value]</code> item inside any iterable handed to them, or a
list tested with <code>in</code> against :py:meth:<code>~multidict.MultiDict.items</code>, is
changed by another thread; a call that catches the list shrinking under it
now raises :py:exc:<code>RuntimeError</code> -- by :user:<code>rodrigobnogueira</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
<a href="https://redirect.github.com/aio-libs/multidict/issues/1437">#1437</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed a data race on the free-threaded build where a retired hash table's
reader count used relaxed atomics, letting a lock-free <code>get()</code>/<code>getone()</code>/
<code>__getitem__()</code> read race a concurrent free of that table. The reader-exit
decrement and the drain's free check now use release/acquire ordering
instead -- by :user:<code>asvetlov</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
<a href="https://redirect.github.com/aio-libs/multidict/issues/1481">#1481</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed a free-threaded build bug where two threads calling <code>update()</code>,
<code>merge()</code>, or <code>__setitem__()</code> on the same key at the same time could lose
the key entirely instead of just racing on which value wins. A decref of the
replaced value could transiently suspend the writer's critical section,
letting a second writer for the same key observe the first writer's
in-progress entry as absent and, once both settled, mistake it for a stale
duplicate and delete it. Every such decref is now deferred until the writer
has released its critical section, so the window can no longer open.
<code>setdefault()</code> had an unrelated instance of the same blind spot (it could
insert a duplicate rather than recognizing an in-flight key), fixed alongside
it -- by :user:<code>asvetlov</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
<a href="https://redirect.github.com/aio-libs/multidict/issues/1483">#1483</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed a free-threaded build bug where <code>getall()</code> and the <code>items()</code>/
<code>keys()</code>/<code>values()</code> equality path could raise <code>KeyError</code> or report a
present, never-deleted key as missing. A concurrent <code>update()</code>/<code>extend()</code>/
<code>__setitem__()</code> call can have its critical section transiently suspended
(a decref triggering a blocking allocator call) while an entry is marked as
part of its own bookkeeping; a reader landing in that window used to treat
the mark as &quot;not found&quot; instead of &quot;still there, in flight&quot; -- by
:user:<code>asvetlov</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em></p>
</li>
</ul>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
</blockquote>
<p>... (truncated)</p>
</details>

<li>
<p>Fixed a data race on the free-threaded build where a retired hash table's
reader count used relaxed atomics, letting a lock-free <code>get()</code>/<code>getone()</code>/
<code>__getitem__()</code> read race a concurrent free of that table. The reader-exit
decrement and the drain's free check now use release/acquire ordering
instead -- by :user:<code>asvetlov</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
<a href="https://redirect.github.com/aio-libs/multidict/issues/1481">#1481</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed a free-threaded build bug where two threads calling <code>update()</code>,
<code>merge()</code>, or <code>__setitem__()</code> on the same key at the same time could lose
the key entirely instead of just racing on which value wins. A decref of the
replaced value could transiently suspend the writer's critical section,
letting a second writer for the same key observe the first writer's
in-progress entry as absent and, once both settled, mistake it for a stale
duplicate and delete it. Every such decref is now deferred until the writer
has released its critical section, so the window can no longer open.
<code>setdefault()</code> had an unrelated instance of the same blind spot (it could
insert a duplicate rather than recognizing an in-flight key), fixed alongside
it -- by :user:<code>asvetlov</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
<a href="https://redirect.github.com/aio-libs/multidict/issues/1483">#1483</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed a free-threaded build bug where <code>getall()</code> and the <code>items()</code>/
<code>keys()</code>/<code>values()</code> equality path could raise <code>KeyError</code> or report a
present, never-deleted key as missing. A concurrent <code>update()</code>/<code>extend()</code>/
<code>__setitem__()</code> call can have its critical section transiently suspended
(a decref triggering a blocking allocator call) while an entry is marked as
part of its own bookkeeping; a reader landing in that window used to treat
the mark as &quot;not found&quot; instead of &quot;still there, in flight&quot; -- by
:user:<code>asvetlov</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em></p>
</li>
</ul>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
</blockquote>

Updates `werkzeug` from 3.1.8 to 3.1.9
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/releases">werkzeug's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>3.1.9</h2>
<p>This is the Werkzeug 3.1.9 security fix release, which fixes security issues and bugs but does not otherwise change behavior and should not result in breaking changes compared to the latest feature release.</p>
<p>PyPI: <a href="https://pypi.org/project/Werkzeug/3.1.9/">https://pypi.org/project/Werkzeug/3.1.9/</a>
Changes: <a href="https://werkzeug.palletsprojects.com/page/changes/#version-3-1-9">https://werkzeug.palletsprojects.com/page/changes/#version-3-1-9</a>
Milestone: <a href="https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/milestone/46?closed=1">https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/milestone/46?closed=1</a></p>
<ul>
<li><code>safe_join</code> on Windows does not allow special devices names with empty ADS markers on NTFS. <a href="https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/security/advisories/GHSA-g6x2-hccm-hh4m">https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/security/advisories/GHSA-g6x2-hccm-hh4m</a></li>
<li><code>ProfilerMiddleware</code> uses <code>profiling.tracing</code> on Python 3.15. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3207">#3207</a></li>
<li><code>uri_to_iri</code> and <code>iri_to_uri</code> preserve empty username, password, and port 0. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3189">#3189</a></li>
<li>Improve performance of <code>parse_options_header</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3231">#3231</a></li>
<li>Improve performance of <code>parse_etags</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3231">#3231</a></li>
<li>Improve performance of <code>parse_cookie</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3231">#3231</a></li>
<li><code>get_host</code> also checks that the port is in the valid range. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3236">#3236</a></li>
<li>The <code>int</code> URL converter returns a 404 instead of 500 error when the value is longer than <code>sys.get_int_max_str_digits()</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3237">#3237</a></li>
<li>Improve debugger PIN generation from cgroup data inside Podman. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3245">#3245</a></li>
<li><code>Authorization</code> parsing <code>basic</code> auth disallows non-base64 characters.  <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3248">#3248</a></li>
<li><code>application/x-www-form-urlencoded</code> form data is no longer limited to <code>max_form_memory_size</code>, only <code>max_content_length</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3251">#3251</a></li>
<li><code>LimitedStream.readinto</code> does not resize the buffer when it reads less than the remaining size. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3253">#3253</a></li>
<li>Rules with 10 or more converters in a single part assign matched values correctly. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3254">#3254</a></li>
<li>The invalid <code>Range</code> suffix length <code>-0</code> is no longer accepted. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3255">#3255</a></li>
</ul>
</blockquote>

2e:Tcbb,Stacked on #34041, which is stacked on #34038. Review only the last commit, `c0d5d0e`.

## Problem

After the facilitator moves the payer's money on-chain, `X402PaymentRequestsService.settle()` marks the row `confirmed` and only then writes creator earnings, as separate non-transactional statements. On the app path that is four writes: `addPurchaseEarnings`, a `purchase_share` row, `apps.total_creator_earnings` and the redeemable credit.

If any of those writes throws (a connection reset, a failed statement), the row is already `confirmed`. Every retry hits the `status === "confirmed"` early return. Nothing reconciles x402 earnings, so the seller is never credited for money that arrived. A partial failure also leaves the four app projections disagreeing with each other.

## Fix

- **Confirm step:** the new `confirmSettlement` confirms the row, releases the settle claim, merges `{payer, settlement}` and sets `metadata.settlementPending = true`, all in one statement.
- **`completeSettlement` (earnings and callbacks):**
  - Records earnings, keyed by the payment ID.
  - Then `clearSettlementPending` atomically clears the flag. Only the caller that clears it sends the `paid` callbacks.
  - A retry, or a concurrent already-settled read, therefore completes the work without crediting twice or sending a second `paid` callback.
- **Repair on retry:** `alreadySettled()` runs `completeSettlement` when the flag is set. Rows confirmed before this change have no flag and are never re-credited.
- **App path:** now uses the repository's atomic, idempotent `addEarnings` → `applyCreatorMovement` pattern, the same one `app-credits` uses. It writes one redeemable ledger entry, then `app_earnings`, the `purchase_share` row and the `apps` aggregates in a single transaction keyed by that ledger ID.
  - Platform revenue delta is `0`. x402 never booked platform revenue on `apps`, so this is unchanged.
- **Non-app path:** `addEarnings` returning `success: false` (sub-unit dust) is logged instead of being silently ignored.

## Evidence Gate

| Check | Result |
| --- | --- |
| New tests on #34041 head `1cc8a4f` | 2 fail. After a one-off `addEarnings` failure, a retry leaves the creator's redeemable balance and ledger at **0** although $5 settled on-chain. After a projection failure, the old writes cannot detect or repair it |
| Same file with this fix | 11/11 pass (credited exactly once, one `paid` callback, flag cleared, legacy confirmed rows not re-credited) |
| Crypto PGlite and unit suites (late-paid, retired-miniapp, confirm-expired, malformed-json, amount-codes) + new | 39/39 pass |
| `redeemable-earnings-fail-closed.test.ts` | 3/3 pass |
| Biome on changed files | clean |
| `tsc --noEmit` (cloud/shared, cloud/api) on changed files | no errors |

2f:T6bc,Scheduled morning briefs now render source-bound Calendar events once in the owner's local time. Timed ranges show their date and timezone, preserving cross-day and DST offsets. All-day events retain their civil date, render as “all day,” and enter the correct owner's-day selection/counts rather than being shifted by UTC-midnight conversion.

33:T1267,# Relates to

Closes #34097

# Problem

The entity-graph writes in `plugins/plugin-sql/src/base.ts` bind caller objects through drizzle's default `JSON.stringify` (or a hand-built `JSON.stringify(...)::jsonb`) with no check for text PostgreSQL jsonb cannot store. A NUL character or a lone UTF-16 surrogate in agent settings, entity/room/world/task/relationship metadata, component data, or a cache value fails in the driver: some writes surface the raw `22P02`/`22P05` `DrizzleQueryError`, others a generic `DB_*` wrapper — and the drizzle `Failed query` message embeds the bound parameters, so the caller's content lands in any log that records the error. The memory path already rejects such text with the typed `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_NUL` / `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_SURROGATE` errors.

# Change

1. **`assertJsonbStorable` (new, `sanitize-json.ts`, re-exported from `utils.ts`):** scans the exact `JSON.stringify` output the bind will carry — where NUL can only appear as the `\u0000` escape and a lone surrogate only as a `\ud800`–`\udfff` escape (literal backslashes are doubled, so escape scanning is exact; paired surrogates serialize as raw astral text) — and throws the same typed errors as the strict sanitizer, before the bind and carrying no content. Per the issue's constraint it is **text-only**: it applies none of `serializeJsonb`'s structural budgets (1 MiB bytes / 10,000 nodes / depth 64), which would silently become new hard limits on component, task, and agent data that have none today.
2. **Guards at every listed write:** `createAgent`, `updateAgent`, `createEntities`, `updateEntity`, `createComponent`, `updateComponent`, `patchComponents`, `createRooms`, `updateRoom`, `createWorld`, `updateWorld`, `compareAndSwapWorldMetadata`, `createTask`, `updateTask`, `updatePendingTask`, `patchTaskMetadata`, `createRelationship`, `updateRelationship` (metadata plus tags, which `independentRelationshipEvidence` re-embeds via `JSON.stringify`), `setCache`. All batch variants funnel through these singulars. `compareAndSetCache` is intentionally untouched: its snapshot encoder in `@elizaos/core` already rejects before the bind with the CAS-specific typed `CACHE_CAS_INVALID_VALUE` contract pinned by `cache-cas.real.test.ts`.
3. **Typed rejection survives storage-fault wrappers:** the `DB_*` J2 catch blocks rethrow `isUnsupportedJsonTextError` unchanged — an inadmissible caller payload is input validation (same category as the existing duplicate-id passthrough), not a storage fault, so the actionable code reaches the caller.

# Validation (real execution, isolated worktree at this branch)

- **Red on unfixed `origin/develop` `65621da3a4`:** the new `jsonb-text-guard.real.test.ts` (real PGlite via `createIsolatedTestDatabase`, no mocked adapters) fails exactly as the issue's table predicts — raw `Failed query: update "entities" …` / `Failed query: insert into "tasks" …` for the direct binds and `DB_UPDATE_FAILED` / `DB_UPSERT_FAILED` / `DB_INSERT_FAILED` wrappers for the caught ones, 29 failures.
- **Green at this head:** 31/31 in the new suite — all 9 acceptance writes × {NUL, lone surrogate} reject with the typed code, the diagnostic contains neither `Failed query` nor the rejected content, the remaining write paths reject too, and the controls pin the no-regression surface: benign metadata writes, paired surrogates still write, and a 1.2 MB component `data` blob still writes (proving the guard stayed text-only and no sanitizer budget leaked in).
- **No regressions in the owning suites** (real PGlite, `vitest.real.config.ts`): sanitize-json consumers plus entity/agent/room/world/world-metadata-cas/relationship/component/cache/task/memory — 153/153 across 10 files; plus `cache-cas`, `task-wake-atomic`, `task-scheduled-at-write-guards` — 9/9. Default unit lane: 27 files, 184/184.
- `bunx tsc --noEmit -p src/tsconfig.typecheck.json` clean; `bunx @biomejs/biome check` clean on the four touched files.

37:T1736,# Relates to

Fixes #34097. Follow-up to #34093 / #34094, which cover memory-content replacement, atomic-publication heads, and `updateLogs`. The other entity-graph jsonb writes in `plugins/plugin-sql/src/base.ts` still bind caller objects through drizzle's `JSON.stringify`. A NUL or a lone surrogate fails in the driver as raw `22P02` / `22P05`, or wrapped in a generic `DB_*_FAILED`, and the `Failed query` message includes the bound value.

# Change

`assertJsonbStorableText` (`plugins/plugin-sql/src/sanitize-json.ts`) walks a value the way `JSON.stringify` does, including `toJSON` results and keys, and throws `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_NUL` or `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_SURROGATE`. It does not rewrite the value and it does not apply `serializeJsonb`'s depth, node, or byte caps, so these writes gain no new size limit.

The check runs before the bind in:

- `createAgent`, `updateAgent`
- `createEntities`, `updateEntity`
- `createComponent`, `updateComponent`, `patchComponents`
- `createRooms`, `updateRoom`
- `createWorld`, `updateWorld`, `compareAndSwapWorldMetadata`
- `createTask`, `updateTask`, `updatePendingTask`, `patchTaskMetadata`
- `createRelationship`, `updateRelationship`
- `setCache`

Batch and upsert methods call those, so they get the same check. Where a `catch` would otherwise wrap the rejection in `DB_INSERT_FAILED` or `DB_UPDATE_FAILED` (`createAgent`, `updateAgent`), the typed error is rethrown.

`compareAndSetCache` already rejects this text inside `encodeCacheCasValue`, before the bind, so it is unchanged.

38:Tcee,Stacked on #34038 (its commit is the first one here). Review only the second commit, `1cc8a4f`.

## Problem

`X402PaymentRequestsService.settle()` calls `x402FacilitatorService.settle()` with no claim on the request. Two settles carrying different valid authorizations (a double-click, or an x402 client retrying with a fresh nonce while the first call is still waiting up to 300s for the receipt) both reach the facilitator and both broadcast:

- **Payer:** charged twice on-chain. The second transfer is untracked: `markAsConfirmed` overwrites the first `transaction_hash`.
- **App-scoped requests:** `addPurchaseEarnings`, `apps.total_creator_earnings` and the `purchase_share` row each increment twice. Only the redeemable credit is deduplicated by `sourceId`.
- **Expiry during an in-flight settle:** if the request lapses mid-settlement, a second settle takes the expiry branch, marks the row `expired` and fires an `"expired"` callback. The first settle then confirms it.

## Fix

- **`claimSettlement(id, until)`:** an atomic `UPDATE … WHERE status='pending' AND no active claim`. It writes `metadata.settleClaimedUntil`. settle takes the claim after validating the payload and before calling the facilitator. The lease is `maxTimeoutSeconds` (default 300) plus 60s.
- **Claim not won:** if the row is confirmed, settle returns the idempotent `alreadySettled` response. If it is pending, settle returns `409 settlement_in_progress`. Otherwise it returns 410. No failure callback fires, because the request has not failed.
- **Facilitator returns `success: false`:** the claim is released, so the payer can retry with a corrected authorization.
- **Facilitator throws:** the claim is kept until it lapses. The transfer may already be broadcast (for example, a receipt timeout), so we never over-pay.
- **`markAsExpired`:** also skips rows with an active claim. The expiry branch returns 409 instead of expiring under an in-flight settle.
- **Success path:** the final metadata write (`{...metadata, payer, settlement}` from the pre-claim read) drops the claim key.
- **No schema migration:** the claim lives in the existing `metadata` jsonb.

39:T129c,## Bug

`VerifyRuntimeArtifact` (`packages/os/native/ota-trust/apk_runtime.go`) reads `assets/runtime-payload.tsv`, an inventory path left over from a retired downstream layout. The shared Android packager, `stageAndroidRuntimeInventory` in `plugins/plugin-native-agent/native-host/android-runtime-inventory.mjs`, writes `assets/agent-runtime.inventory` instead, and `RuntimeBundleStore` reads that file on device. As a result, every real native-runtime APK fails with "runtime inventory missing". The tests only passed because their synthetic fixtures used the old name, and the shared test policy hardcoded a product-specific `senior-care-runtime-v1` header.

Fixing only the path does not make real APKs pass. I ran the verifier against the two real native-runtime release APKs from a downstream host (launcher and standalone, about 139 MB each). Both then failed with `unlisted runtime asset`, because the packager deliberately leaves two kinds of file under `assets/agent/` that are not in the inventory:
- **Host-excluded directories** (`excludedAgentDirectories`, e.g. `models/`). The agent service reads these straight from APK assets.
- **Original `.tar` / `.tar.gz` archives.** The packager re-addresses these as listed `runtime-blobs/<sha256>.bin` entries. `RuntimeBundleStore` never extracts the original paths.

## Fix

- **Path.** The verifier now reads `assets/agent-runtime.inventory`. The packager exports this name as `ANDROID_RUNTIME_INVENTORY_ASSET`, and `RuntimeBundleStore` reads the same file.
- **Header.** The header still comes from the host. `stageAndroidRuntimeInventory` takes a validated `format` (default `eliza-runtime-v1`), and the host passes that same value to the packager, to `RuntimeBundleStore` and as its policy's `runtimeInventoryHeader`. Nothing in the shared package names a product any more.
- **Exclusions.** Host policy moves to **schema 2**, which adds a required `runtimeExcludedAgentDirectories`. It must match the packager's `excludedAgentDirectories`; each entry is a safe relative path, the list is unique, and it holds at most 64 entries. Schema-1 policies fail closed in both Go and `composeTrustSource`. An unlisted file under `assets/agent/<dir>/` is allowed only when `<dir>` is excluded. Unlisted `assets/runtime-blobs/*` entries are still always rejected.
- **Archives.** An unlisted agent archive is allowed only if its packager destination (`<name>.tar.gz`, or `<name>.tar` + `.gz`) appears in the inventory as a blob destination. It is covered by the whole-APK digest.
- **Shared test fixture.** `testdata/runtime-apk` is real `stageAndroidRuntimeInventory` output. The new Go test `TestPackagerProducedRuntimeInventory` builds an APK from it and verifies it. It also covers rejection of the legacy path, unlisted agent files, unlisted blobs, archives without a listed blob, `models.txt` when only the `models/` directory is excluded, tampered assets, a different header, and a missing exclusion. A new plugin native-host test regenerates the inventory from the same inputs and asserts it is byte-identical, so if the packager and verifier drift apart, CI fails.

No compatibility is kept for `runtime-payload.tsv` / `senior-care-runtime-v1`. The only producer of that layout was a retired downstream build, and the verifier only checks candidate APKs, which current packaging never writes in the old layout.

## Downstream migration

Host policy changes to: `"schema": 2`, `"runtimeInventoryHeader"` set to the value the host passes to the packager and `RuntimeBundleStore` (normally `eliza-runtime-v1`), and `"runtimeExcludedAgentDirectories"` set to the host's packager exclusions.

## Tests run (local, macOS arm64)

- `packages/os/native/ota-trust`, Go 1.27.1: `go test -race ./...` passes; `go vet ./...` is clean.
- `node --test scripts/compose-source.test.mjs scripts/build-android.test.mjs`: 6/6 pass.
- `plugins/plugin-native-agent`, `node scripts/test-native-host.mjs` (Node 24.15.0, JDK 21): 28/28 Node tests pass, including the new format and fixture-parity tests. All Java native-host suites pass, including the Node-to-Java `RuntimeInventoryInterop`.
- Biome 2.5.14 `check` is clean on the changed JS.
- `TestActualPackagedRuntime` with `OTA_NATIVE_APK_DIRECTORY` pointing at the two real downstream native-runtime release APKs (`eliza-runtime-v1`, exclusions `arm64-v8a,x86_64,riscv64,models`): both pass. With `[]` exclusions both are rejected, as expected. Before this change both were rejected.
- Not run: the repo-wide `bun run verify`, because dependencies were not installed in this checkout.

These tests do not show that an OTA installation, device-owner provisioning, recovery or real-device acceptance works.

3a:T1095,Fixes #34092

# Relates to

On develop (base `65621da3a42`), `PATCH /api/lifeops/entities/:id` (`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/routes/entities.ts`) and `PATCH /api/lifeops/relationships/:id` (`routes/relationships.ts`) work in three steps:

1. Read the record (`store.get`).
2. Wait for the request body (`readJsonBody`).
3. Upsert the merge of the record read in step 1.

Meanwhile the presence bridge (`presence-signal-bridge-service.ts`, on MESSAGE_RECEIVED/SENT/REACTION_RECEIVED) folds in identities (`observeIdentity`), records interactions (`recordInteraction`), and bumps `interactionCount` (`relationshipStore.observe`), and the retire route writes the same edge. Anything written between steps 1 and 3 was reverted:

| What changed during the PATCH | Effect of the stale write-back |
| --- | --- |
| A new identity was folded in | `EntityStore.upsert` replaces identities wholesale, so its rows are deleted. |
| `interactionCount` went 5 → 6 | It goes back to 5. |
| `lastInboundAt` was updated | It goes back to the old value. |
| The edge was retired | It comes back `active`, because the stale `status` wins. |

# Change

- Both routes read the body first, so the record is no longer held across the slow body read.
- `EntityStore.patch(entityId, mutate)` reads, merges, and writes in one store operation. That is a single transaction on the durable-record (SQLite) path.
- `RelationshipStore.patch(relationshipId, mutate)` does the same under the edge's existing per-edge serialization (`edgeOperation`, which `observe` already uses), so it is serialized against `observe` on both backends.
- Both return null for an unknown id, and the routes answer 404 as before.
- On the raw-SQL entity path, `operation()` has no transaction. There the window shrinks from the request-body wait to two back-to-back queries. A full PostgreSQL entity transaction is outside this change.

An unknown entity gets 404 with no write. |
| `plugin-relationships/__tests__/store-patch.records.test.ts` | Real in-memory SQLite runtime | `patch` receives the current entity or edge after an intervening write and observation, and returns null for unknown ids. |

3b:T2682,# Relates to

Queue-cleared audit finding (bug tier) in the same filter-before-limit family as #33909 (triage search), #33922 (chat history), and #33926 (X DM direction), all merged. No open issue tracks this one; the failure below was reproduced on current `origin/develop` before any branch was created.

**Reproduced failure:** a `missedOnly` inbox view (LifeOps inbox missed tab: unreplied 24h+ messages with priority score >= 50) loses genuinely missed messages whenever recent non-qualifying traffic fills the newest-first `limit` window:

- `buildInboxFromMessages` (`plugins/plugin-inbox/src/inbox/aggregate.ts`) trimmed newest-first rows to `limit` and only then applied the missed filter, so 30 recent messages hid a 48h-old scored unreplied invoice (`expected [] to deeply equal [ 'discord:missed-invoice' ]` pre-fix).
- The cache read (`cacheReadLimitFor`) and the refresh source read (`cacheWarmLimitFor`) were capped at the display `limit`, so missed rows beyond the window never reached the builder (`expected 24 to be 72` pre-fix).
- Refresh LLM scoring selection (first pass of `buildInboxWithLlm`) only scored the newest-`limit` window, so older missed candidates stayed unscored and invisible (`expected [] to deeply equal [ 'gmail:ext-refresh-missed-invoice' ]` pre-fix).

# Change

- `buildInboxFromMessages`: apply `llmScores` to the collected set, then apply the missed filter **before** the display-limit trim when `missedOnly` is set. Thread groups are built from the surviving window. Non-`missedOnly` behavior is unchanged (same trim, same scores, same groups). Removed the now-unused `isMissedThreadGroup` helper.
- New `MISSED_ONLY_WINDOW_FACTOR = 3` (mirrors the `limit * 3` candidate paging in `message-fetcher.ts`): `missedReadWindow()` widens the cache read (`cacheReadLimitFor`), the refresh source read (`cacheWarmLimitFor`), and the first-pass LLM scoring selection (`buildInboxWithLlm`) to `limit * 3` when `missedOnly` is set. Unset `limit` stays uncapped; an explicit `cacheLimit` still wins via `Math.max`.
- Material risks: missed-view refresh scores up to 3x messages (LLM cost only on user-initiated missed views); thread groups in a missed view now contain only qualifying messages (previously a surviving group kept its non-missed members, which also leaked non-missed rows into the cache via `flattenInboxMessages`).

# Testing

Reproduced first (3 new tests fail on the base commit), then fixed (all green):

- `bun <vitest> run --config ./vitest.config.ts --root plugins/plugin-inbox inbox-aggregate` → **21 passed (18 existing + 3 new)** on the PR head after rebasing onto `origin/develop` (`65621da3a4`).
- Full owning-package suite: **32 files passed, 1 skipped; 295 tests passed, 2 skipped**.
- `tsc --noEmit -p plugins/plugin-inbox/tsconfig.json` → exit 0.
- `biome check` on both changed files → clean.

New regression tests (`test/inbox-aggregate.real-runtime.test.ts`, real PGlite runtime + contract-true seams, deterministic TEXT_SMALL stub):

1. `missedOnly spends the limit window only on messages that can qualify` — 30-message flood + old scored invoice + old scored replied message, `limit: 24` → only the invoice survives, in messages and thread groups.
2. `missedOnly widens the cache read so a recent flood cannot hide missed messages` — cache-only mode asserts the read asked for 72 (`24 * 3`) and returns the scored cached missed row with its stored score.
3. `missedOnly scores missed messages beyond the newest-limit window on refresh` — Gmail seam honoring `maxResults`, refresh mode returns the old invoice scored 90.
4. `missedOnly grouped view keeps whole threads, not just the missed subset` (added with `bb01f5a03b`) — mixed A/B thread (A: 30h-old scored unreplied; B: 2h-old scored, not yet missed), grouped `missedOnly` view keeps both members, `latestMessage.id` is B and `totalCount` is 2.

3c:T12dc,# Relates to

Existing approved [Assistant routines #31022](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/31022).

# Change

The task extractor could return an old action format to a JSON-only parser. This PR requests JSON on every task-create path and retains the generic parser behavior for other callers. Specific saved-fact lookup uses advertised memory references or targeted history first. Complete original history remains available when needed.

A real timed reminder extraction later omitted its destination decision because the guidance said to omit it. Guidance, examples and repair now require nativeProjection for create mode. An omitted destination in a timed reminder or alarm uses the existing repair path. A genuinely unknown null remains conservative. The unmanaged path uses the same parser. Explicit alert bodies remain separate from short titles.

# Why this implementation

This reuses the existing provider option, parser and bounded repair. Missing-field repair still uses the same two-call bound. No new I/O, model stage, provider parameter or history limit is added. Consent, source binding, receipts and completion checks stay in place.

# Scope and testing

This update changes exactly three existing files: extract-task-plan.ts, its existing test and task-extraction-projection.test.ts. They match reviewed extractor sourcee907b17c72b745b2cbaff5be5c95bddcb66ac29e. The other eight files retain their previous bytes; the eleven-file PR scope is unchanged. Clock context and reminder editor/delivery changes remain in their separate drafts.

Meaningful baseline failure and99 owning cases passed for this extraction correction. The tests include actual provider-conversion prompts, missing-field repair, conservative null decisions and restored/unmanaged paths. Earlier observed JSON and saved-fact runs remain historical supporting evidence, not proof that all future requests avoid repair or full-history restoration.

## How to test

1. Compose this draft with the named reminder and Clock dependencies where their behavior is needed. Use pinned Node24.15.0/Bun1.4.2.
2. Request a timed app reminder with a separate alert body. Check that extraction emits nativeProjection=in_app_only and retains the body.
3. Return a first extraction result that omits nativeProjection. Check that the existing repair is used once; no extra stage is added.
4. Return null for a genuinely unknown destination. Check that it remains unknown and does not authorize an effect.
5. Ask for a specific saved fact, then exhaustive history. Check that targeted reads and complete restoration remain available.

3d:Tfa8,# Relates to

Follow-up to #33937. The built-in `X402BudgetTracker` caps per service and per day for one client. An agent runtime that wants a budget per agent, per room or per task has to keep it outside the client, through the payment callbacks. Today that can't be done correctly. `onBeforePayment` and `onPaymentComplete` share no identity, so concurrent payments can't be matched, and nothing fires when an approved payment fails. A hold taken in `onBeforePayment` therefore can't be released.

# Change

- `onBeforePayment(req, url, attempt)` and `onPaymentComplete(log, attempt)` receive `attempt = { paymentId }`, one id per payment attempt (`crypto.randomUUID()`).
- New `onPaymentFailed(req, url, error, { paymentId, transferAttempted })`. It fires when a payment that `onBeforePayment` approved (or that ran without it) throws before completing. `transferAttempted` follows the client's existing rule: a submitted transfer may still land, so a cautious budget keeps its hold. A throwing callback doesn't hide the payment error.
- `X402PaymentAttempt` is exported from `sdk/x402` and `sdk`.

Backward compatible. Existing callbacks ignore the extra argument, and a declined `onBeforePayment` doesn't call `onPaymentFailed`. The internal tracker is unchanged.

# Testing

New `plugins/plugin-wallet/src/sdk/x402/client.payment-attempt.test.ts`, 5 tests through `X402Client.fetch` with the mocked wallet-core used by the existing x402 tests:

- The same `paymentId` reaches `onBeforePayment` and `onPaymentComplete`.
- An approved payment that fails before any transfer is reported, with that id and `transferAttempted: false`.
- A failed submitted transfer is reported with `transferAttempted: true`.
- A declined payment isn't reported.
- An external budget keyed by `paymentId`, under 6 concurrent payments with one failure, ends with nothing held, spend equal to the completed payments, and spend within its limit.

```
vitest run src/sdk/x402/   Tests 24 passed (24), 3 consecutive runs
```

Three of the new tests fail without the change. `biome check` (2.5.14) is clean on `src/sdk/x402/` and `src/sdk/index.ts`. Strict `tsc` on the changed files reports no errors.

3e:T9271,# Relates to

https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/34084

`McpService` only requests the first `tools/list`, `resources/list`, and
`resources/templates/list` page. A paginated server therefore connects with an
incomplete capability catalog: tools and resources after the first page are
missing from the model-facing MCP projection and resource-template inventory.

The [MCP pagination contract](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/blob/main/schema/2025-06-18/schema.ts)
defines opaque cursors and continuation through `nextCursor`. Duplicate checks
covered the full open PR inventory, recent service history, and prior MCP paging
PRs; #32833 addresses cloud tool-index pagination, and #24044 addresses marketplace
paging, not these SDK server discovery lists.

# Change

Collect every discovery page before marking the server connected. Preserve order,
empty intermediate pages, opaque/empty-string cursors, and existing schema compatibility.
Repeated cursors raise `MCP_PAGINATION_CURSOR_REPEATED`; an endless stream of fresh
cursors raises `MCP_PAGINATION_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` after the explicit 1,000-page budget
per list. Neither failure publishes a partial connected catalog. The per-server
error boundary reports the typed error and allows healthy siblings to load.

A sticky empty cursor remains a protocol failure: interpreting repeated `""` as
completion cannot establish that discovery is complete. This behavior is tested
for all three lists and documented, alongside the explicit page limit.

Diff: **271 production/test lines**, plus 8 documentation lines and 2 dependency
metadata lines; six files, +275/-6. The testing dependency is development-only;
the lockfile only adds that workspace dependency. Prepared with Codex assistance.

# Testing

Windows, pinned Node 24.15.0 / Bun 1.4.2.
Final checked head: `d6689090a4beba33ab18b4bfc8140548d3d79c70`; freshly fetched base: `65621da3a424f8fcdce760db5c9c414117082e18`.

| --- | --- |
| `bun install --frozen-lockfile` | exit 0 |

Full package: **23 files, 163 passed, 0 failed, 3 skipped**. The existing opt-in
npx/registry cases account for the skips; all **18 new real-stdio regressions** run
unconditionally. They cover normal and empty catalogs, empty intermediate pages,
repeated/cyclic/sticky-empty cursors, later-page RPC failures, and endless cursors
on each list. Endless discovery rejects explicitly while a healthy sibling stays
connected and the service load resolves.

The original 12-case harness on upstream production produced 10 failed / 2 passed;
its full baseline suite produced 147 passed / 10 failed / 3 skipped. The review's
new endless cases produced three 5-second timeouts at `4a225940` before the budget
was implemented; all 18 regressions then passed with the review patch. These are
separate comparisons, not a claim that the new 18-case harness ran on clean upstream.

Environment: process-local npm registry override; Git GNU tools with `MSYS=noglob`
and `TURBO_ENV_MODE=loose`. `ELIZA_SKIP_FUSED_INFERENCE_SETUP=1` skips the optional
local inference backend. Live validation uses the real workspace text handlers,
the real RECENT_MESSAGES provider, the registered MCP plugin/action, and isolated
SQLite persistence. Unsupported DeepSeek embeddings are not registered.
