# Evidence Details

The live run exercises runtime registration, database-backed conversation context,
the real model selection/argument/synthesis code, and an actual SDK child process.
It does not inject tool selection or replace runtime/model methods. The last-page
tool response records all nine discovery requests. The assertions verify real
tool/reply database readback. This is a synthetic local MCP server with genuine
DeepSeek inference, not a deployed external MCP server or full app-host session.

<details><summary>Reproduction harness (local credential file is excluded)</summary>

```typescript
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { execFileSync } from "node:child_process";
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import { writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { ChannelType, ModelType, type Memory, type UUID } from "@elizaos/core";
import { createSQLiteTestRuntime } from "../../packages/testing/src/sqlite-adapter.ts";
import mcpPlugin from "../../plugins/plugin-mcp/src/index.ts";
import { mcpAction } from "../../plugins/plugin-mcp/src/actions/mcp.ts";
import { McpService } from "../../plugins/plugin-mcp/src/service.ts";
import { handleTextLarge, handleTextSmall } from "../../plugins/plugin-openai/models/text.ts";
import { recentMessagesProvider } from "../../plugins/plugin-assistant/src/features/basic-capabilities/providers/recentMessages.ts";

const key = process.env.OPENAI_API_KEY?.trim();
assert.ok(key, "Configured DeepSeek key required");
assert.equal(new URL(process.env.OPENAI_BASE_URL!).hostname, "api.deepseek.com");
const head = execFileSync("git", ["rev-parse", "HEAD"], { encoding: "utf8" }).trim();
const requests: Array<Record<string, unknown>> = [];
const callbacks: unknown[] = [];
const originalFetch = globalThis.fetch;
const redact = (text: string) => text.replaceAll(key!, "[REDACTED]");
const save = (name: string, value: unknown) => {
  const serialized = JSON.stringify(value, null, 2) + "\n";
  assert.ok(!serialized.includes(key!), "Credential must never enter evidence");
  writeFileSync(new URL(name, import.meta.url), serialized);
};
globalThis.fetch = Object.assign(async (input: Parameters<typeof fetch>[0], init?: RequestInit) => {
  const url = new URL(typeof input === "string" || input instanceof URL ? input : input.url);
  if (url.hostname !== "api.deepseek.com") return originalFetch(input, init);
  const request = typeof init?.body === "string" ? JSON.parse(init.body) : {};
  const started = new Date().toISOString();
  const response = await originalFetch(input, {
    ...init,
    signal: init?.signal ? AbortSignal.any([init.signal, AbortSignal.timeout(90000)]) : AbortSignal.timeout(90000),
  });
  const body = await response.clone().json();
  requests.push({ started, apiHost: url.hostname, path: url.pathname, httpStatus: response.status,
    requestedModel: request.model, returnedModel: body.model,
    messages: request.messages, visibleResponse: body.choices?.map((choice: any) => ({ content: choice.message?.content, finishReason: choice.finish_reason })),
    usage: body.usage });
  return response;
}, { preconnect: originalFetch.preconnect });

const fixture = fileURLToPath(new URL("../../plugins/plugin-mcp/__tests__/fixtures/paginated-server.mjs", import.meta.url));
const runtime = createSQLiteTestRuntime({
  character: {
    name: "MCP pagination live evidence", bio: "Synthetic local MCP scenario with real DeepSeek inference",
    system: "Follow the current task instructions exactly. Return concise visible answers.",
    settings: { mcp: { maxRetries: 1, servers: { pages: { type: "stdio", command: "node", args: [fixture, "pages"] } } } },
  },
  plugins: [
    { name: "deepseek-text", description: "Real workspace OpenAI-compatible text handlers; DeepSeek does not provide embeddings", models: { [ModelType.TEXT_LARGE]: handleTextLarge, [ModelType.TEXT_SMALL]: handleTextSmall } },
    mcpPlugin,
  ],
  logLevel: "fatal",
});
let receipt: Record<string, unknown>;
try {
  for (const setting of ["OPENAI_BASE_URL", "OPENAI_SMALL_MODEL", "OPENAI_LARGE_MODEL"]) runtime.setSetting(setting, process.env[setting]!);
  runtime.setSetting("OPENAI_API_KEY", key!, true);
  runtime.registerProvider(recentMessagesProvider);
  await runtime.initialize();
  const service = await runtime.getServiceLoadPromise("mcp") as McpService;
  const catalog = service.getProviderData();
  assert.deepEqual(Object.keys(catalog.data.mcp.pages.tools), ["tool-0", "tool-2"]);
  const roomId = randomUUID() as UUID;
  const entityId = randomUUID() as UUID;
  const worldId = randomUUID() as UUID;
  await runtime.ensureConnection({ roomId, entityId, worldId, source: "mcp-live-evidence", type: ChannelType.DM, userName: "synthetic-user" });
  const message: Memory = { id: randomUUID() as UUID, entityId, roomId, worldId, agentId: runtime.agentId, content: { text: "Call MCP tool tool-2 on server pages with an empty argument object and report its JSON receipt.", source: "mcp-live-evidence" } };
  await runtime.createMemory(message, "messages");
  assert.equal(await mcpAction.validate(runtime, message), true);
  const result = await mcpAction.handler(runtime, message, undefined, { action: "call_tool" }, async (content) => { callbacks.push(content); return []; });
  assert.equal(result.success, true);
  assert.equal(result.data?.toolName, "tool-2");
  const transport = JSON.parse(result.data!.output as string);
  assert.equal(transport.tool, "tool-2");
  assert.equal(transport.requests.length, 9);
  assert.ok(requests.length >= 3);
  assert.ok(requests.every((request) => request.httpStatus === 200));
  const memories = await runtime.getMemories({ tableName: "tools", roomId, count: 10 });
  assert.ok(memories.some((memory) => memory.content.text?.includes('"tool":"tool-2"')));
  const replies = await runtime.getMemories({ tableName: "messages", roomId, count: 10 });
  assert.ok(replies.some((memory) => memory.entityId === runtime.agentId && memory.content.text));
  assert.equal(execFileSync("git", ["rev-parse", "HEAD"], { encoding: "utf8" }).trim(), head);
  receipt = { head, completedAt: new Date().toISOString(), provider: "deepseek", configuredModel: process.env.OPENAI_LARGE_MODEL,
    scope: "Real SQLite AgentRuntime + registered MCP plugin + real workspace text handlers + model-selected MCP action + stdio SDK transport + SQLite tool/reply readback; no app-host/message-router session claimed.",
    userRequest: message.content.text, discoveredTools: Object.keys(catalog.data.mcp.pages.tools), modelRequests: requests, transport, actionResult: result, callbacks,
    persistenceReadback: { toolMemories: memories.length, assistantReplies: replies.filter((memory) => memory.entityId === runtime.agentId).length }, credentialRedacted: true };
  save("live-deepseek-receipt.json", receipt);
  console.log(JSON.stringify({ head, livePassed: true, modelRequests: requests.length, tool: transport.tool, discoveryRequests: transport.requests.length, toolMemories: memories.length }));
} catch (error) {
  save("live-deepseek-failure.json", { head, at: new Date().toISOString(), error: redact(error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)), modelRequests: requests });
  console.error("Live DeepSeek MCP validation failed; details are in the redacted local failure receipt.");
  process.exitCode = 1;
} finally {
  globalThis.fetch = originalFetch;
  await runtime.stop();
}

```
</details>

and DeepSeek requests. Embedding/local inference behavior is outside this validation.

model request correctly selected no tool. That attempt is retained separately and
excluded from passing evidence. The completed run uses the real provider and makes
three HTTP-200 requests. Credentials remain in the ignored local file.

Historical root runs at `4a225940` include one interruption with no exit receipt
and an exit-4 Cloud API worker dry-run. They are not counted as passing; that exact
worker command subsequently passed, and root verify later passed at that head.
The table above records only the new final head's actual results.

One final-head root attempt was interrupted without a final exit receipt. It is
preserved separately and is not counted as passing. The replacement runs the
same `bun run verify` with the repository-supported `RUN_TURBO_CONCURRENCY=1`
override to serialize compilers, matching the hosted memory policy.

requested changes. The endless-cursor budget and healthy-sibling regression are
implemented in `d6689090`; sticky-empty coverage/documentation were added. The
strict repeated-cursor error is intentionally retained rather than guessing an
end-of-list marker. A later comment confirms the cap finding is closed and keeps
the sticky-empty compatibility objection open.

body explicitly cites reviewed head `4a225940` and the original 12-case suite.
It is not evidence of independent execution of the new 18-case suite or review
of the entire final-head patch. The sticky-empty compatibility disagreement
remains for maintainer/reviewer consideration.

3f:T4a4,Native hosts can already store pending enrollment and revocation journals in `LocalCredentialBroker`, but the shared JavaScript client exposes only the active credential slot. Consumers currently have to copy the private HTTP transport to use those pending operations.

Adds `createLocalPendingCredentialStore` through the same validated, deadline-bound, non-redirecting transport as `createLocalCredentialStore`. Each factory fixes its wire operation names; pending reads/writes/clears cannot address the primary slot. Response validation, credential-safe errors, and the rule against retrying an uncertain write acknowledgement remain shared. Product token policy, encrypted-store identity and revocation decisions remain with the host.

Validation: 25 real-loopback cases cover both factories, malformed replies, redirect refusal, stalled response bodies, lost acknowledgements and primary/pending isolation. Native host tests, plugin tests (24 passed), package typecheck and Biome pass. Full `bun run verify` also passed at `33ecff7f57decf1af87e1a9c215a330049fd806a`. This does not change the native broker, expose a renderer capability, or qualify a consumer's live sign-in journey.
40:T163d,# Relates to

No existing issue found. Stream `Go Live` and `Stop Stream` request failures were caught and discarded, leaving the stream page unchanged with no explanation or retry feedback. GitHub issue and PR searches for this failure mode returned no duplicates on 2026-10-06.

# Change

- Keep stream action failures visible in an accessible `role="alert"` surface.
- Preserve the server-reconciled live/offline state after a failed action.
- Clear the action error only when the operator retries, so background status polling cannot erase it.
- Add rendered regression coverage for failed start, retry success, and failed stop.
- Add a production-browser flow that observes the failed HTTP response and captures desktop/mobile evidence.

Risk is limited to the stream status surface. Successful start/stop behavior is unchanged.

41:T1670,# Relates to

Queue-cleared bug audit finding in the same filter-before-limit family as #33922, but in the shipped `packages/agent` HTTP inbox route rather than `plugin-inbox`. No open issue or pull request covers this route; the failure below was reproduced on current `origin/develop` before the fix.

`GET /api/inbox/messages` asked storage for only `limit * 3` raw memories, then removed reaction events, blank content, disallowed sources, and duplicate projections. A run of newer non-renderable rows could therefore make the endpoint return an empty or short page while older eligible connector messages existed.

# Change

- Page room-scoped and aggregate memory reads until the requested number of renderable, deduplicated messages is available or storage is exhausted.
- Preserve source discovery across pages, including source-less rows seen before a later row identifies the room's connector.
- Accumulate reaction events across the scanned pages and attach them to the projected messages after collection.
- Stop safely when an adapter repeats a page or repeats every identified row, so an adapter that ignores `offset` cannot loop forever.
- Keep the scan uncapped by an arbitrary history window: the public `limit` counts eligible messages, not raw storage rows.

Material risk: a request can perform additional paged storage reads when invalid or filtered rows dominate recent history. Reads stop as soon as the response is full, storage ends, or a non-advancing adapter is detected.

# Testing

Reproduced first with a real SQLite runtime and real HTTP server: six newer blank Discord memories filled the old raw window for `limit=2`, so both room-scoped and aggregate requests returned `{ messages: [], count: 0 }` even though two older visible memories existed. The same test now returns both older messages through both route shapes.

- `bun --conditions=eliza-source node_modules/vitest/vitest.mjs run --config packages/agent/vitest.config.ts packages/agent/test/inbox-message-window-http.test.ts` — **1 file, 1 test passed**.
- `TMPDIR=/tmp bunx -p node@24.15.0 node packages/agent/scripts/run-vitest-batches.ts` — **123/123 isolated test files passed**.
- `bun run --cwd packages/agent typecheck` — passed.
- `bun run --cwd packages/agent build` — passed.
- `bun run --cwd packages/agent lint:check` — exit 0 (31 existing warnings in untouched mobile stubs/tests); Biome on both changed files is clean.
- `bun run verify` under Node 24.15.0 / Bun 1.4.2 — **298/298 workspace tasks passed**, followed by all repository audits. An initial high-concurrency run was killed by the host while typechecking `cloud-e2e`; the immediate retry completed successfully.

<!-- This risks section must be filled out before the final review and merge. -->

# Risks

**Low–medium.** The only behavior change is on `/cloud/*` and registered
`cloud`/`admin` group routes **when a host passes `cloudManagementElement`**.
Every existing consumer passes no such prop, so `cloudManagementElement ??
AppCatchAllRoute` keeps today's routing bit-for-bit. One deliberate composition
change mirrors `develop`: individually registered cloud/admin routes now honor
the same pending/error/retry registration barrier as the `/cloud/*` wildcard
(#29283) instead of mounting the app immediately — required so a registered
route cannot mount the agent app before the management renderer is ready.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

A signed-in zero-credit account opening Cloud Account Settings on the hosted
production renderer boots the full agent app: once private Cloud surfaces
register, `/cloud/account` matches its registered route and `CloudRouteElement`
renders `AppCatchAllRoute`, mounting the chat shell with the
"Message Eliza — waking up…" composer while no agent runtime exists.

`develop`/`staging` already fix this with the standalone `ManagedCloudPage`
passed as `cloudManagementElement`, but that boundary never reached the
production composition, and the open staging promotion (#33708) carries 2,313
unrelated commits held on Cloud certification.

This PR backports **only the boundary** to `main`:

- `CloudRouterShell` accepts `cloudManagementElement` and renders it after the
  `CloudManagementSessionGate` and the pending/error/retry private-registration
  barrier — on the `cloud/*` wildcard and on individually registered
  cloud/admin routes (`PrivateCloudAppRoute`).
- `packages/app/src/main.tsx` and `packages/app/src/public-web-entry.tsx` pass
  the standalone `ManagedCloudPage` (lazy, web-build-only, mirroring
  `develop`'s wiring verbatim).

No subscription, funding, legacy-redirect, or settings-anchor changes from
`develop` are included.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue) — hosted Cloud account
management renders independently of agent boot, matching the already-merged
`develop` behavior.

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. The boundary
matches the existing `ManagedCloudPage`/router composition already documented
in source headers on `develop`.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/ui/src/cloud/shell/CloudRouterShell.private-registration.test.tsx` →
describe `"hosted Cloud management without the agent runtime"` (6 new tests).

## Detailed testing steps

None: Automated tests are acceptable, plus the red/green reproduction below.

- As a signed-in account, open `/cloud/account` on the hosted renderer
  - before: the agent app mounts (chat shell, "waking up…" composer)
  - after: the standalone `ManagedCloudPage` renders; the agent app never mounts
- Signed-out `/cloud/account` still redirects to
  `/login?returnTo=%2Fcloud%2Faccount`; private-load failure still shows
  "Console unavailable" with working Retry; the pending `aria-busy` barrier
  still holds before either renderer mounts.

Verification run in this environment (filtered frozen install, `bun` 1.4.2,
vitest 4.1.10):

  behavior predates the fix); all pass after restoring the fix.

  30/30 passed with the new wiring.
- Typecheck: `packages/ui` `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` clean; `packages/app`
  typecheck shows no diagnostics in the two changed files (remaining output is

  real Vite dev server, `VITE_PLAYWRIGHT_TEST_AUTH=true` with the repository's
  canonical seeded steward session): on this branch the hosted renderer boots,
  `/cloud/account` authenticates through the real session gate, and the

43:T1151,Fixes #34076

# Relates to

`HealthDomain.resolveHealthWindow` (`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/domains/health-service.ts`) defaults the window's `endDate` to `new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)`, the UTC date. Health samples, however, are keyed by the provider's local day: `local_date` for Fitbit and Oura (`lifeops/repositories/health.ts`). The same window is used by:

- `getHealthSummary`, which backs the HEALTH action (`plugins/plugin-health/src/actions/health.ts`), `GET /api/lifeops/health/summary`, and the lifeops-health provider;
- `syncHealthConnectors` and `forceSync`.

On develop (base `65621da3a42`), for an owner in Asia/Tokyo at 2026-10-06 08:00 JST (still 2026-10-05 UTC):
- `endDate` was `2026-10-05`, so today's samples (`local_date 2026-10-06`) were excluded;
- the HEALTH "today" reply fell back to another day's summary, or reported "have not synced daily summaries yet";
- `forceSync` never fetched today.

West of UTC the same happens in the evening. #31264 and #33110 fixed the HealthKit bridge and Google Fit paths; this connector window was still on UTC.

# Change

- `resolveHealthWindow` takes the owner's local date for its default end.
- Both callers resolve it with `resolveOwnerTimeZone(runtime, now)`, then `getZonedDateParts` and `getLocalDateKey`.
- The start date stays civil-date arithmetic from that end. Explicit `startDate`/`endDate` requests are unchanged.
- Resolving inside the domain covers the action, the route, the provider, and sync at once.

Follow-up (`48e7920`), from review. Reads bounded by instants read dates as UTC days: workouts use `start_at <= endDate T23:59Z`, and the Strava, Oura and Withings sync use instant bounds. Ending the window on the owner's date dropped a west-of-UTC owner's evening after UTC midnight.

- Those reads now end on the later of the owner's date and the UTC date (`instantEndDate`).
- Samples and sleep, which are keyed by `local_date`, keep the owner's date.
- `forceSync`, and the summary that sync returns, pass the request's own dates, so both resolve the same window.

`health-service.status.test.ts`:
- Its `makeDomain` now provides the context's `runtime`, which production always has, with a configurable `TIMEZONE`.
- New case "ends the default summary window on the owner's local date": with Asia/Tokyo and the clock at `2026-10-05T23:00Z`, a 2-day summary queries `2026-10-05 … 2026-10-06`.

New `health-service.window.test.ts` (fake clock, stubbed repository, connector sync replaced at the plugin-health boundary):
- In Los Angeles at 23:30 PDT, the default summary's workouts window ends `2026-10-06`, so a 19:00 PDT workout is inside it, while samples end `2026-10-05`.

44:Td41,Fixes #34069

# Relates to

`GET /api/inbox/messages` (`packages/agent/src/api/inbox-routes.ts`, `loadInboxMessages`) reads one window of `limit * 3` rows. The window comes from one of two calls:
- `getMemoriesByRoomIds` across every agent room in non-internal worlds. This is a single global newest-first `LIMIT`, not a per-room one.
- `getMemories` for a selected room.

Only after that read does it drop rows outside the requested `sources`, Discord reaction events, and empty texts, then slice to `limit`. The constant's own comment calls the 3× margin "a reasonable margin".

On develop (base `65621da3a42`), a busy connector ahead of the requested source therefore left nothing to return:
- `?sources=telegram&limit=20`, behind 60 or more newer Discord or web-chat rows, answered `{ messages: [], count: 0 }` although older Telegram messages exist.
- A selected Discord room with many reactions returned fewer than `limit` messages.

The response carries no truncation flag, so in both cases the shortfall looked like a complete answer.

# Change

- The record-building pass (source inference, reaction, and empty-text filters, dedupe) is unchanged. It is wrapped in a function so it can run over everything read so far.
- The read pages older with the same `limit * 3` window and `offset`, until `limit` records survive or a short page ends history.
- Ordering, the final `slice(0, limit)`, and Discord sender-profile enrichment are unchanged.

- Result: the 3 Telegram messages, newest first.

45:T10d4,# Problem and result

The feature template asks for any idea. The contributor guide does not clearly require MVP scope or proof of useful improvement. This can direct contributors toward work that maintainers will reject.

This change makes the contribution rules prominent in the README, agent guide, all four issue templates, the issue chooser, and the PR template. The feature template now asks for an approved MVP gap. A new feature needs human discussion, maintainer approval, and updates to the product requirements document (PRD) and minimum viable product (MVP) plan before implementation work begins.

PRs must show a useful improvement, use and simplify existing code, explain alternatives, and provide results from real end-to-end tests. Every user interface (UI) change requires an uploaded explainer video, proof of the changed flow, and detailed test steps. All issues and PRs must use ASD-STE100 Simplified Technical English. The guides state that maintainers will close unnecessary work and apply contributor penalties.

Scope: the repository owner's explicit contribution-policy request. This changes no product requirement or product feature.

# Why this implementation

I reviewed the current root guides, each issue template, the PR template, and the existing evidence checker. The README holds the full policy. Each entry point highlights the rules and links to that policy.

A separate policy file would duplicate the existing contributor guide. A new automated gate would add code without proving the value of a contribution. This change uses the current guides and preserves all existing evidence markers.

# Validation

- All four issue templates and the issue chooser pass YAML parsing.
- All 19 local Markdown links and section links in the changed guides resolve.
- The GitHub contributor-guide link returns HTTP 200.
- The existing PR evidence checker passes its 14 built-in cases.
- All evidence row markers and the commit marker are unchanged.
- `git diff --check` passes.
- Bun 1.4.2 and Node 24.15.0 were used for `bun run verify`. It stopped because this new worktree does not have the `yaml` dependency installed. Full repository verification is not complete. Translation gaps printed earlier in that run were warnings.

This is a documentation and template change. No runtime or new unit tests were added. Dependency installation was not run in this worktree.

# How to review

1. Open the README contribution section. Check that scope, proof, reuse, test results, videos, writing, and penalties are required.
2. Open AGENTS.md and each issue template. Check that the important notice is visible and links to the contribution rules.
3. Read the feature template. Check that it asks for approved MVP work and requires human approval for new features.
4. Read the PR template. Check that it requests the implementation rationale, real test results, and detailed test steps. Check that UI video evidence cannot be marked as not applicable for a UI change.
5. Run `node packages/scripts/check-pr-evidence.ts --self-test` with Node 24.15.0. Expect all 14 cases to pass.

46:Tfb4,Fixes #34037

# Relates to

The `CLOUD_ACCOUNT` provider (`plugins/plugin-elizacloud/src/cloud-providers/cloud-account.ts`) serves a per-runtime snapshot of credit balance and hosted agents, stale-while-revalidate: any cached value renders, and expiry only schedules a background refresh. Its header states the invariant: "every mutating cloud action must call `invalidateCloudAccountCache(runtime)`". On develop (base `4f0416b29be`) only API-key creation does.

| Case | Result on develop |
| --- | --- |
| `POST /api/cloud/agents` (`client.createAgent`) | The new agent is missing from the planner's account context until the TTL passes and one more turn runs. |
| `POST /api/cloud/agents/:id/shutdown` (`client.deleteAgent`) | The deleted agent keeps being narrated as running. |
| Sign-out, then sign-in to another org on the same runtime | The cache entry survives. The next turn renders the previous org's balance and agents, labelled with the new org's id (`render(..., auth.getOrganizationId())`). |
| `elizacloud_credits` (`credit-balance.ts`) | Reuses the shared snapshot via `getCachedAccountSnapshot`, which has no age check at all. |

# Change

- **Agent create/shutdown routes** (`cloud-routes-autonomous.ts`): call `invalidateCloudAccountCache` after the upstream call succeeds. The cache key is now typed as the runtime object, so the route's `RuntimeLike` needs no cast.
- **Org scoping** (`cloud-account.ts`): each snapshot records the organization it was fetched for. The provider and the shared read ignore a snapshot from any other organization. This covers every sign-in/sign-out path without having to thread invalidation through each one.
- **Shared read TTL:** `getCachedAccountSnapshot` returns the snapshot only within its 60 s TTL.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (base `4f0416b29be`).
- [x] Ran `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation (JSDoc).

New `__tests__/unit/cloud-account-freshness.test.ts` uses the existing real-SDK loopback cloud harness:

- **Create/shutdown:** warm the provider (2 agents), then drive the real `handleCloudRoute` for agent create and shutdown through a loopback HTTP request. The next `get()` reflects 3 agents, then 2.
- **Account switch:** warm the cache under `org-first`, then switch to `org-second` with a new balance. `CLOUD_ACCOUNT` renders `(org org-second): $3.50`, and `elizacloud_credits` no longer shows `$12.34`.

The harness `makeRuntime` gains an optional `organizationId` reader.

Results:
- Against develop's sources, both tests fail (`expected '… $12.34…' to contain '3 hosted agents'`; `'ElizaCloud credits: $12.34' not to contain '12.34'`).
- The plugin-elizacloud suite passes 82 files / 771 tests.
- `bun run verify` passes on this head: 298/298 Turbo typecheck/lint tasks plus every audit, exit 0.

47:T4a71,# Relates to

Closes #34093
Refs #33124

Three jsonb writes in `plugins/plugin-sql/src/base.ts` skipped the shared sanitizer that #33124 extended to lone surrogates: the parent update when `publishMessageContentSegments` runs in replace mode, the head of `compareAndSwapMemoryPublication`, and `updateLogs`. A NUL or a lone UTF-16 surrogate in those writes reached PostgreSQL and failed with the raw `22P05` / `22P02` driver error. That error's message also carries the bound content. The matching create paths return typed codes instead. The public `runtime.replaceMessageMemoryContent` reaches the first write, and plugin-assistant calls it after attachment enrichment, incoming-hook rewrites and translation.

# Contribution provenance

<!-- contribution-attribution:v1 -->
<!-- attribution-row:ai-assistance -->
- AI assistance: `yes`
<!-- attribution-row:models -->
- Model(s) used: `anthropic/claude-opus-5-5`
<!-- attribution-row:client -->
- Agent tooling: `Claude Code`
<!-- attribution-row:skill-revision -->
- Skill path: `elizaOS/army@46fa4776ba6bd0df2e7236c95a3c77e69cd1db99:skills/contribute-to-eliza`
<!-- attribution-row:status -->
- Provenance status: `self-reported`

# Change

1. **Message content replacement.** In replace mode, `publishMessageContentSegments` now serializes the replacement parent with `serializeJsonb(content, { memoryContent: true })` (`base.ts:2128`). That is the policy `insertMemoryInTransaction` applies to a `messages` row in create mode. The update binds the result as `::jsonb` (`base.ts:2226`). The serialization runs first in the replace branch, before the row lock, the expected-content check and any segment delete or insert. Bad text is therefore refused with `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_NUL` / `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_SURROGATE` and nothing is written.
2. **Atomic publication head.** `compareAndSwapMemoryPublication` serializes the head's content and metadata with the same table-based selection `insertMemoryInTransaction` uses: `serializeDocumentJsonb` for a `document_fragments` head, otherwise `serializeJsonb` with `memoryContent: true` and `documentText` set only for `documents`. Metadata goes through `serializeJsonb(metadata ?? {})` (`base.ts:7937-7944`). It replaces the two `JSON.stringify` calls. This runs before `withDatabase`, so no dependency row is staged, and PostgreSQL's `withRetry` does not retry a rejection that depends only on the input.
3. **Log updates.** `updateLogs` binds a new body the way `log()` binds one: `JSON.stringify(sanitizeJsonObject(body))` cast to `jsonb` (`base.ts:7895`). NUL is stripped and lone surrogates become U+FFFD. The sanitized value has to be bound as text: `sanitizeJsonObject` builds null-prototype objects, and drizzle's `.set()` reads `Object.getPrototypeOf(value).constructor` on a plain value, which throws for those.

Behaviour that follows from reusing the create-path policy:

- An invalid replacement or head is refused before the conflict check. A call that is both invalid and stale now gets the typed error instead of `conflict`.
- `updateLogs` also gets `log()`'s structural budget: depth 64, 10,000 nodes, 1 MiB serialized. A body that `log()` would refuse fails with `SQL_JSON_SANITIZE_UNBOUNDED`, and the stored body is unchanged. No production code in the repository passes a body to `updateLogs`; plugins reach it through `runtime.updateLogs`.

No schema, migration, route, or public type changes. Both `PgDatabaseAdapter` and `PgliteDatabaseAdapter` inherit these methods from `BaseDrizzleAdapter` and override none of them.

# Testing

Environment: Bun 1.3.14 on this machine (the repository pins 1.4.2), Node 24.15.0, `bun install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts`. This is not CI parity. One commit, `55dcb5a3b8`, on `origin/develop` `65621da3a4`. The real suites ran on PGlite through an untracked local config, not committed: it merges `src/vitest.real.config.ts` with `resolve.conditions` and `ssr.resolve.conditions` set to `["eliza-source"]`, and with `retry: 0`. A clean worktree has no built `@elizaos/core`, so the committed config cannot resolve it (`Failed to resolve entry for package "@elizaos/core"`). With only `resolve.conditions` set, Vitest 5 still failed to resolve it. Commands run from `plugins/plugin-sql/src`.

| Control | Command | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |

| RED, logs, `base.ts` from `origin/develop` | same | 1 failed, 13 passed (14): the new test, raw `22P05` |

What the new tests check:

- `memory.real.test.ts:336` runs `runtime.replaceMessageMemoryContent` with a lone high surrogate and a NUL in `text`, the same two in a nested `content.metadata` value, and a replacement over the 64 KiB inline limit with a lone surrogate in `thought`. That last one would stage segment rows before the parent update. Each call must fail with the typed code, leave the stored message `toEqual` its previous value, and leave the memory table holding only that message. An emoji replacement is then stored unchanged. The replace path writes only `content`, so the metadata case is the content's nested `metadata` object.
- `memory.real.test.ts:367` runs `compareAndSwapMemoryPublication` with a fresh dependency on every call. It covers a lone surrogate and a NUL in head `content.text` and in a head metadata value, for both a first publication (insert) and a revision swap (update). 48:T7fa,## Problem

`X402PaymentRequestsService.settle()` reads the request, and if `expires_at` has passed it calls `cryptoPaymentsRepository.markAsExpired(id)`. That update matched on `id` only. If a concurrent settle confirms the request after the first read (the facilitator settled just inside the deadline), the expiry branch overwrites `status='confirmed'` with `expired` and fires an `"expired"` failure callback after the `"paid"` callback already went out. The seller then sees a paid request reported as expired, and the credited earnings no longer match the row status.

## Fix

- `markAsExpired` now updates only `status='pending'` rows. It is used only by x402 settle; the cron uses the separately guarded `persistPaymentFailure`. `markAsConfirmed` is unchanged, because OxaPay late-paid settlement needs to confirm an expired row (`crypto-late-paid-settlement.pglite.test.ts`).
- When the guarded expire matches nothing, settle re-reads the row. If it is confirmed, settle returns the existing idempotent `alreadySettled` response and sends no failure callback. The response building is moved into `alreadySettled()`.

## Evidence Gate

| --- | --- |
| New `x402-settle-expiry-race.pglite.test.ts` on develop source | 2/3 fail: confirmed row flipped to `expired`; `markAsExpired` overwrote a confirmed row |
| Same test with fix | 3/3 pass |
| Related PGlite suites (late-paid settlement, retired miniapp) + new | 9/9 pass |
| Biome on changed files | clean |
| `tsc --noEmit` (cloud/shared, cloud/api) on changed files | no errors |

49:T2453,# Relates to

`chunkSlackText` (exported from `@elizaos/plugin-slack`, also used by `markdownToSlackMrkdwnChunks`) sends empty code blocks when a split lands next to a fence. Same class as #34062 / #34063 (core `chunkMarkdownText`) and #34082 (Discord `chunkDiscordText`); Slack has its own fence-parity chunker, so it needs its own fix.

On `develop`:

- `chunkSlackText("Lead paragraph text.\n\n```\nconst x = 1;\n```", 30)` → first message is ``"Lead paragraph text.\n\n```\n\n```"`` (empty block).
- ``"```\n" + "a"*20 + "\n\n```\nOutro."`` at 30 → second message is ``"```\n```\nOutro."`` (the reopened fence closes immediately).
- At small limits the break can cut through a ```` ``` ```` marker (``"wwww\n`"`` + ``"``\nab..."``), so the fence-parity state flips and prose after the block is wrapped in code.
- At `maxChars` ≤ 9 inside a code block the reopened ```` ```\n ```` uses the whole `maxChars - 4` budget (or the one remaining unit is half a surrogate pair), so the loop makes no progress and never returns. It grows memory until the process is killed.

Scope: nothing inside `plugin-slack` calls `chunkSlackText` (the service uses its own `splitMessage`), and the default limit is 40,000, so this is a correctness fix for the exported helper rather than a measured live-send bug.

# Change

`chunkSlackText` (`plugins/plugin-slack/src/formatting.ts`):

1. **Opening side.** If the chunk's last fence opens a block with only whitespace after it, the chunk ends before that fence and the block starts in the next chunk. If only whitespace precedes the fence, that whitespace is skipped instead of being sent as its own message. If the chunk *starts* at the fence (the first chunk, or any reopened chunk), the whitespace is skipped and nothing is emitted for it; the block is then reopened, or its closer consumed, as in the next item.
2. **Closing side.** After a split inside a block, if only whitespace and the block's closer remain, the closer is consumed instead of reopening the block. A whitespace-only remainder is dropped.
3. **Marker splits.** A break point that falls inside a ```` ``` ```` marker moves back to the marker's start.
4. **No infinite loop.** If a reopened fence leaves no room for any code, the chunker throws `SLACK_CHUNK_LIMIT_TOO_SMALL` (the existing fail-closed error, with an accurate message) instead of looping.

The `maxChars` bound and fence balance are unchanged. Only whitespace-only fence remnants and redundant fence lines are removed; non-whitespace content is unchanged.

4a:Taa6,Fixes #34182

## Problem

`VideoService.getTranscript` parses the first `subtitles.en` variant that has a `url` as SRT. yt-dlp's YouTube extractor lists manual subtitles as `json3, srv1, srv2, srv3, ttml, srt, vtt`, so that first variant is `json3`. `parseSRT` then drops two lines of the JSON document and returns the rest as the transcript, so every YouTube video with manual English subtitles gets raw JSON as its transcript text.

`parseSRT` also always drops the first two lines of each block. A WebVTT cue without an identifier loses its first text line. And because it splits only on `\n\n`, whitespace-only separators or bare-CR line endings let cue numbers and `-->` timings leak into the text.

## Fix

- `manualSubtitleUrl()` prefers the `srt` variant, then `vtt`, then any variant with a url. That last fallback covers extractors that omit `ext` and keeps the #29564 behaviour of skipping url-less leading entries.
- `parseSRT` normalizes `\r\n`/`\r`, splits cues on blank or whitespace-only lines, and keeps only the lines after each block's `-->` timing line. Blocks with no timing line (WEBVTT header, NOTE, STYLE) are dropped.
- `YtDlpSubtitleTrack` gains the optional `ext` field yt-dlp already returns.

## Tests

- `repro-transcript.test.ts`: a YouTube-shaped variant list selects `fmt=srt` and yields `hello there`. A list without `srt` falls back to `vtt` and keeps cues that have no identifier.
- `video.test.ts`: `parseSRT` keeps WebVTT cues without identifiers and splits on whitespace-only and bare-CR separators. The existing CRLF test still passes.

On develop the 4 new tests fail: the transcript is `"events": [ { "tStartMs": 1000, ... } ] }`, the first VTT cue is dropped, and timings leak. With the fix they pass.

4b:T1ac7,# Relates to

Queue-cleared audit follow-up on the assistant messaging path (no open issue carries this defect; reproduced locally before branching). Same warm-cache contract family as #34018/#33909 reviews: one identical `MESSAGE listInbox` request must honor `sinceMs` regardless of cache temperature.

# Change

The `MESSAGE` listInbox action documents `sinceMs` as *"Only include messages received at or after this timestamp"*. The cold path enforces that floor inside the connector adapters (`triage()` forwards `sinceMs` into `adapter.listMessages`). The cached path served the warm `TriageService` store with only the source filter and `rankScored` re-order — `sinceMs` was silently dropped:

- **Before:** with a warm store, `listInbox { sinceMs: T }` returned unread rows received **before** `T`. The same request returned filtered rows when the cache was cold and unfiltered rows when it was warm — the documented floor depended on cache temperature.
- **After:** the cached branch applies the same floor (`receivedAtMs >= sinceMs`) after the re-order; the cold path and the no-`sinceMs` behavior are unchanged.

No public surface changes: `parseListInboxParams` still validates `sinceMs` as an optional number, and the action result shape is unchanged.

4c:T2388,# Relates to

Discord replies that contain a fenced code block sometimes arrive as a message ending in an empty code box, followed by a second message that reopens the block. Measured with production defaults (`chunkDiscordText(text)`: 2000 characters / 17 soft lines) over 690 prose-plus-code reply shapes, **23 (3.3%)** send an empty ```` ```ts ```` block.

Example at defaults: message 1 ends with ``"...Sentence number 13 explains a step.\n\n```ts\n```"``, and message 2 is ``"```ts\n  const value0 = compute(0);\n..."``.

Same class as #34062 / #34063 (core `chunkMarkdownText`). Discord has its own line-based chunker, so it needs its own fix. All Discord send paths use it: `service.ts`, `slash-commands.ts`, `discord-interactions.ts`.

# Change

`chunkDiscordText` (`plugins/plugin-discord/messaging.ts`):

1. **Opening side.** The chunker reserves room only for the synthetic closer, so a fence opener could be the flushed buffer's last line. `flush()` now leaves that opener out of the flushed chunk; the existing reopen logic starts the next chunk with it.
2. **Closing side.** After a reopen, if only whitespace follows the reopened opener when the original closing line arrives, the reopened fence and that closing line are dropped. The previous chunk already closed the fence. The same applies when the original closing line itself forces the flush: it is longer than the reserved synthetic closer (trailing whitespace, or a longer marker), so the flushed chunk's synthetic closer replaces it. It also applies at end of input.
3. **Unterminated trailing opener.** An input that ends in a bare fence opener no longer sends a trailing empty block; `flush()` and the final flush share `withoutTrailingOpener`.

An authored empty code block that fits inside one chunk is preserved; only fence remnants created by a split are dropped. A test pins this.

Code content, the 2000-character limit, and fence balance are unchanged. Only whitespace-only fence remnants and redundant fence lines are removed.

Not changed here: Slack's `chunkSlackText` has the same pattern; it is fixed separately in #34089. iMessage trims whitespace at chunk boundaries; that's a separate behavior.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop`.
- [x] Ran `bun install` and `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation. (N/A: internal chunking behavior.)

Results:
- Two new tests in `plugins/plugin-discord/__tests__/messaging-chunking.test.ts`:
  - The 690-shape sweep at production defaults. Each chunk must stay within 2000 characters with balanced fences and no empty block; code lines must arrive intact and in order.
  - 5 closing-side shapes (blank line before the closer, `"``` "`, `"~~~  "`, ```` "`````" ```` after ```` ````md ````, `"```\t"` after `"\n \n"`) at `maxChars` 20..80. Each chunk must stay within the limit with balanced fences and no empty or whitespace-only block; the code must be preserved.
  - An unterminated trailing opener (``"x"*50 + "\n\n```ts"`` at `maxChars` 20) sends no code block.
  - An authored ```` ```\n   \n``` ```` block inside one chunk is kept byte-for-byte.
  - `develop`: 7 cases fail. `4ecc5ddf` (first revision): 1 fails (the trailing opener). This branch: 21/21 pass.
- Wide fuzz (26,136 cases: 6 fence styles × 4 pre-closer gaps × 3 body sizes × 3 widths × `maxChars` 20..140, two fences per text):

  | | Empty blocks | Unclosed fences | Over-limit chunks | Lost content |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `develop` | 5,791 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
  | this branch | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

<details><summary>Defaults sweep script (run from the repository root with Bun)</summary>

```ts
import { chunkDiscordText } from "./plugins/plugin-discord/messaging.ts";
const emptyBlock = (c: string) => /(^|\n)```[^\n]*\n```(\n|$)/.test(c);
let msgs = 0, bad = 0, ex: string[] | undefined;
for (let intro = 1; intro <= 30; intro++) for (let lines = 3; lines <= 25; lines++) {
  const prose = Array.from({ length: intro }, (_, i) => `Sentence number ${i} explains a step.`).join("\n");
  const code = Array.from({ length: lines }, (_, i) => `  const value${i} = compute(${i});`).join("\n");
  const text = `${prose}\n\n\`\`\`ts\n${code}\n\`\`\`\n\nThat is the whole change.`;
  const chunks = chunkDiscordText(text);
  msgs++;
  if (chunks.some(emptyBlock)) { bad++; ex ??= chunks; }
}
console.log({ replies: msgs, repliesWithEmptyCodeBlock: bad });
console.log(JSON.stringify(ex?.slice(0, 2)));
```
</details>

4d:Tc89,Fixes #34096

# Relates to

`GET /api/database/tables/:table/rows` (`packages/agent/src/api/database.ts`, `handleGetRows`) backs the app's database view: `DatabaseView` → `ElizaClient.getDatabaseRows`. It pages with `LIMIT … OFFSET …`, each page a separate query, over:
- `ORDER BY <sort column> <dir>` when a sort is chosen, with no tie-break;
- no `ORDER BY` at all when unsorted.

On develop (base `65621da3a42`), a sort column with ties (a type, a status, a shared timestamp) is therefore not a total order. PostgreSQL may return tied rows in a different order for each page query, so rows are shown twice and others never. Unsorted pages follow heap order, which changes after `UPDATE`/`VACUUM`; `plugin-sql/src/base.ts` documents the same hazard for its own queries.

Example: paging 120 rows sorted by a two-valued column, 25 per page, shows only 98 distinct rows.

# Change

- The route looks up the table's primary-key columns, using the same `information_schema.table_constraints`/`key_column_usage` join as `GET /api/database/tables`.
- The order becomes `ORDER BY <sort> <dir>, <pk…> <dir>`, or just the primary key when unsorted.
- A table without a primary key falls back to its physical row id (`ctid`).
- Sort validation, search, `total`, and the response shape are unchanged.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (base `65621da3a42`).
- [x] Ran `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation (inline comment).

New `packages/agent/test/database-rows-paging.test.ts` runs on a real PGlite runtime and serves the real `handleDatabaseRoute` over HTTP:
- It creates a 120-row table with a two-valued `kind` column, then updates some rows.
- It pages 25 at a time, sorted by `kind` and unsorted.
- Every id must appear exactly once.

Results:
- Against develop's route, the test fails: `expected 98 to be 120` distinct ids.
- `bun run verify` passes on this head: 298/298 Turbo typecheck/lint tasks plus every audit, exit 0.

4e:T1b9e,# Relates to

Relates to #29597 (honest loading, unavailable, and recovery states).

Reproduction I picked up: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/29597#issuecomment-6009301372 — a rejected `streamGoLive()`/`streamGoOffline()` in the shipped Stream view was silently absorbed: the shared mutation catch discarded the error, reconciled status, and restored the idle panel, leaving "Stream Ready" with no alert. The reproducer verified the failure and the repair shape against `develop@65621da3a4` but removed their temporary edit; no PR existed for this surface (searched open issues and PRs before starting).

# Change

`toggleStream` in `packages/ui/src/components/pages/StreamView.tsx` now publishes a rejected Go Live / Stop Stream mutation error through the existing `statusError` alert panel (`role="alert"`) before reconciling authoritative stream status. The next successful status poll clears the alert, so the error stays visible while the backend is actually broken and disappears once healthy state is re-confirmed.

- No new visual surface: the alert panel markup is the existing status-error JSX, reused unchanged.
- No backend path is touched; the reconciling status read is unchanged.
- Risk is contained to the Stream view's action-error branch; the status-error behavior that already used `statusError` is untouched.

4f:T17e2,# Relates to

Relates to #29597.

Reproduction proof: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/29597#issuecomment-6009361902

# Change

The voice-preset editor now rebases the user's provider/voice selection onto the fresh server-side TTS configuration fetched at save time. This prevents a failed initial config read (or a concurrent settings change) from turning the editor's partial fallback state into a destructive replacement of hidden TTS fields such as ElevenLabs credentials, model, mode, and saved voice.

Adds a rendered regression test that makes the initial config request fail, selects an Edge voice through the real combobox, lets the save-time request recover, and verifies the complete existing ElevenLabs configuration survives in the update payload.

50:Tdee,# Relates to

Same defect class as #34062 / #34063 (core `chunkMarkdownText`), #34082 (Discord plugin `messaging.ts`), and #34089 (Slack `chunkSlackText`), on the cloud Discord delivery path. Filed standalone since no issue tracks the gateway module.

# Problem

`chunkDiscordText` in `packages/cloud/services/gateway-discord/src/discord-text-chunks.ts` split replies at raw 2000-character boundaries. Every live reply longer than Discord's content limit that contains a code fence could be delivered broken:

- A fence opener near the boundary was cut in half: the first message ends with a stray `` `` `` fragment and the next begins with the orphan rest of the marker, so neither renders as a code block.
- A fenced block crossing the boundary was split into two messages with unbalanced fences: the first message renders everything after the opener as code and ends mid-line; the second starts with raw code lines and its trailing fence opens a code block that swallows the following prose.

This module backs three real delivery paths: `gateway-manager.ts` message routing (line 1652), managed reply delivery (line 2691), and `proactive-greeting-delivery.ts` DMs (line 156). Reproduced against the real module on `develop` (`65621da3a4`):

```
case1 chunk1 tail: "xxxxxx``"        // marker split across messages
case1 chunk2 head: "`\nconst "
case2 chunk0: fences=1 balanced=false  // dangling code block
case2 chunk1: fences=1 balanced=false
```

# Change

Delegate the split to the core fence-aware Markdown chunker (`chunkMarkdownText`, already a runtime dependency of this service): a ``` ``` ``` marker is never cut in half, and a split inside a code block closes the fence at the end of the current message and reopens it at the start of the next, so every delivered message stays fence-balanced. Content remains complete; only balanced synthetic fence lines are added, matching the sibling fixes.

The module's own contracts are preserved: the lone-surrogate sanitizer (`toWellFormedUnicode`), the empty-input fast path, the `RangeError` for non-integer or `< 2` limits, `DISCORD_MESSAGE_CONTENT_LIMIT`, and `discordChunkNonce` are unchanged.

# Validation

Real execution in an isolated worktree on this branch (`bun --conditions=eliza-source test` in `packages/cloud/services/gateway-discord`):

- New `tests/discord-text-chunks.test.ts` (5 tests, 1964 assertions): invalid-limit/empty/sub-limit guards, the marker-split case (the whole fence now arrives intact in one balanced message), the crossed-boundary case (every message fence-balanced, code body preserved through the synthetic close/reopen), a paragraph/fence sweep across limits 40–400 and 2000, and unchanged surrogate handling.
- Pre-existing service tests: 13/14 pass; the single failure (`deploy-railway` bundle proof) fails identically on the pristine base commit in this scratch install (missing hoisted deps) — environmental, not a regression.
- `biome check`: clean on both touched files. Scoped `tsc --noEmit` over the two touched files against real core source: zero errors in either.

# Relates to

`GoalsDomain.syncComputedGoalReviewState` (`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/domains/goals-service.ts`) persists a computed review. On develop (base `65621da3a42`) it builds `nextGoal = { ...goal, reviewState, metadata }` from the caller's goal snapshot and writes it through `repository.updateGoal`. That call rewrites every column of `life_goal_definitions` (title, description, status, cadence, `metadata_json`, …) with no version guard.

The snapshot can be old by the time it is written:
- `reviewGoal` (route `POST …/goals/:id/review`, the `LIFE` review action) reads the goal before `evaluateGoalProgressWithLlm`, which takes seconds.
- `getOverview` → `refreshGoalReviewStates` takes one `listGoals` snapshot, then loops over every goal with per-goal queries.

Writes to the same row in that window are reverted. They come from the goal PATCH route (`GoalsService.updateGoal`) and from check-in replies (`GoalsCheckinService.recordCheckinResponse` appends `metadata.checkinLog`). For example, the owner renames "Run 5k" to "Run 10k" or logs a week-2 check-in during a review, and the review writes back "Run 5k" and the week-1 log.

# Change

- New `GoalsRepository.updateGoalReview(agentId, goalId, { reviewState, metadataPatch, updatedAt })` sets only `review_state` and `updated_at`.
- It merges only the given top-level metadata keys into the stored metadata in SQL: `COALESCE(metadata_json, '{}')::jsonb || patch::jsonb`. plugin-goals runs on PostgreSQL/PGlite only.
- `syncComputedGoalReviewState` writes through it, passing just the metadata keys the review changed (`computedGoalReview`, and the semantic review when present).
- `LifeOpsRepository` forwards the new method.
- The audit event and the returned goal are unchanged.

- It stores a goal and reads it (the stale snapshot).

- It runs the real `syncComputedGoalReviewState` with the stale snapshot.
- The stored goal keeps "Run 10k" and both check-ins, and records `reviewState: "at_risk"` and `computedGoalReview`.

52:Te65,Fixes #34081

# Relates to

`computeScreenTimeRange` and `enumerateScreenTimeHistoryDays` (`plugins/plugin-health/src/screen-time/ranges.ts`) compute day boundaries with the host clock: `setHours(0)`, `setDate`, `getDay`, and local getters. `ScreenTimeDomain.getScreenTimeHistory` (`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/domains/screentime-service.ts`) uses them for `GET /api/lifeops/screen-time/history`, and the owner's zone is never consulted.

On develop (base `65621da3a42`), a cloud host running in UTC that reports for an owner in America/Los_Angeles gets:

| Range | What the owner sees |
| --- | --- |
| `range=today` at 16:00 PDT | Starts at 17:00 PDT the previous day, so it includes 7 hours of yesterday |
| `range=today` at 18:00 PDT | Starts at 17:00 PDT today, so it covers 1 hour |
| `this-week` | Starts on the UTC Sunday |
| Every history bucket | A UTC day, not the owner's day |

Desktop installs, where the host zone is the owner's, are unaffected. #12995 moved these helpers from UTC to host-local, not to the owner's zone.

# Change

- Both helpers take an optional `timeZone`. With it, they compute that zone's civil days using plugin-health's `util/time` (`getZonedDateParts`, `buildUtcDateFromLocalParts`, `addDaysToLocalDate`, `getWeekdayForLocalDate`, `getLocalDateKey`), so 23- and 25-hour DST days are exact. Bucket labels format in that zone.
- Without a zone they keep the host's days. That is the user's own zone when the helpers run in the app, so other callers are unchanged.
- `getScreenTimeHistory` resolves the owner's zone (`resolveOwnerTimeZone`) and passes it to both helpers.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (base `65621da3a42`).
- [x] Ran `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation (JSDoc).

New cases in `ranges.localdate.test.ts`, all with the host set to `TZ=UTC`:

| Case | Expected |
| --- | --- |
| `today` at 16:00 PDT, owner in Los Angeles | Starts at `2026-10-06T07:00Z` |
| `this-week` | Starts Sunday Oct 4 00:00 PDT |
| History across the DST change | Buckets begin at `07:00Z` for Oct 31 and Nov 1, and at `08:00Z` for Nov 2 |

Results:
- Both new cases fail against develop's `ranges.ts`.
- plugin-health `src/screen-time` passes 28/28, and the personal-assistant screen-time tests pass 7 files / 136.

56:T738,Fixes #34176.

`searchEntitiesByName` applied `LIMIT` with no `ORDER BY` in both branches and put the raw query into a LIKE pattern. That broke the `IDatabaseAdapter` contract (case-insensitive substring, exact matches first) and made `%`/`_` wildcards.

**Change** (`plugins/plugin-sql/src/base.ts`):
- Empty query: `ORDER BY created_at, id`, the same total order `queryEntities` uses (#33119).
- Substring query: `ILIKE '%' || escapeIlikeLiteral(query) || '%' ESCAPE '\'` (the existing helper used by memory text search), then `ORDER BY <exact case-insensitive name match> DESC, created_at, id`.

**Tests:** new `__tests__/integration/entity-search-order.real.test.ts` (PGlite/Postgres). Rows are inserted in a physical order different from `created_at`. The tests assert: oldest-first pages for empty and substring queries, exact match ahead of older partial matches, and literal `%`/`_`.
- Before (PGlite, develop a853470): 3/4 fail consistently; exact-match fails intermittently (unordered scan order varies) — 3 failed / 1 passed on first run; 4/4 failed on a later repeat.
- After (head 1de7e914ba): 4/4 pass on 5/5 repeat runs (PGlite). Existing entity suites 77/77. Live Postgres (`POSTGRES_URL`, pgvector): 4/4 new + 81/81 entity regression.
- `biome check` on the two changed files: clean. `tsc --noEmit -p src/tsconfig.typecheck.json`: 0 errors.

57:Tda8,Fixes #34065

# Relates to

`RelationshipStore.list` (`plugins/plugin-relationships/src/knowledge-graph/relationship-store.ts`) has two read paths:

- the durable-record path (`records.listRelationships`, used when the adapter supplies `recordStore`, as plugin-sqlite does);
- the raw-SQL path (`ORDER BY updated_at DESC LIMIT n`).

On develop (base `65621da3a42`), both apply `filter.limit` to the read, and only then apply `metadataMatch` and `cadenceOverdueAsOf` in memory.

Overdue edges are, by definition, the least recently updated ones. So `GET /api/lifeops/relationships?cadenceOverdueAsOf=…&limit=N` (`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/routes/relationships.ts`, `parseRelationshipFilter`) returned the N freshest edges, none of them overdue, and answered `{ relationships: [] }` while overdue contacts existed. `metadataMatch` with `limit` had the same order of operations.

# Change

When `metadataMatch` or `cadenceOverdueAsOf` is set:
- both read paths run without the limit;
- the limit is applied to the filtered result, truncating like the SQL path's `sqlInteger`.

Reads without those filters are unchanged and still limit in the query.

# Testing

| Test | Harness | What it checks |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `__tests__/relationship-store.test.ts` (SQL path), new case | In-memory SQL interpreter | The overdue edge updated before three fresh edges is returned for `cadenceOverdueAsOf` + `limit: 2`. |
| `__tests__/relationship-store.test.ts`, existing `metadataMatch … limit: 1` assertion | Same | Previously pinned `[]`, recorded as characterization in #25699. It now expects the matching edge, and the test is renamed to match. |
| New `__tests__/relationship-store.records-limit.test.ts` (durable-record path) | Real in-memory SQLite runtime (`AgentRuntime` plus the declared `@elizaos/plugin-sqlite` adapter) | Same scenario as the new SQL-path case. |

58:Tdfb,Fixes #34074

# Relates to

The `SCREEN_TIME` action (`plugins/plugin-health/src/actions/screen-time.ts`) resolves the owner's zone and computes "today" as the owner's local date (`localTodayKey(timeZone)`, since #31264). It then calls `getScreenTimeDaily({ date })`.

On develop (base `65621da3a42`), `ScreenTimeDomain.getScreenTimeDaily` (`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/domains/screentime-service.ts`) turns that date into the UTC day `${date}T00:00:00Z … T23:59:59.999Z` (`resolveUtcDateWindow`). The local date is therefore read over the wrong 24 hours:

| Owner zone, local time | UTC window covers | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| America/Los_Angeles, 2026-10-06 20:00 PDT | Oct 5 17:00 → Oct 6 16:59 PDT | Tonight's 17:00–20:00 is dropped and yesterday evening is counted. |
| Asia/Tokyo, 2026-10-06 08:00 JST | Starts at 09:00 JST, in the future | "No screen time recorded for 2026-10-06." |

# Change

- `getScreenTimeDaily` takes an optional `timeZone`. With it, the window is that zone's civil day: local midnight to the next local midnight, built with the PA's `buildUtcDateFromLocalParts` and `addDaysToLocalDate`, so 23- and 25-hour DST days are exact.
- Without a zone it keeps the UTC day, so other callers are unchanged.
- The action passes the `timeZone` it already resolves. The two forwarding declarations (`LifeOpsService`, the screen-time mixin) and the plugin-health `ScreenTimeActionService` interface gain the optional field.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (base `65621da3a42`).
- [x] Ran `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation (JSDoc).

New `screentime-service.daily-window.test.ts` records the window `getScreenTimeDaily` reads:

| Input | Window |
| --- | --- |
| Los Angeles, 2026-10-06 | `07:00Z` → `06:59:59.999Z` next day |
| Los Angeles, 2026-11-01 (DST end) | A 25-hour window: `07:00Z` → `07:59:59.999Z` next day |
| No zone | The UTC day |

- The `screen-time.test.ts` action assertions now also pin the zone passed (`Asia/Tokyo`, `UTC`).
- Against develop's service, both zoned cases fail.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
59:T2611,# Relates to

Fixes #34062.

`chunkMarkdownText` emits an empty code block and then reopens the fence in the next chunk in two cases:
- the fence's closing line is longer than `indent + marker` (trailing whitespace, a longer marker, an indented closer, `~~~ `), or
- the fence body ends in blank lines (this happens even with a plain ```` ``` ```` closer).

The chunker reserves room only for the synthetic closer. At some limits the cut therefore lands where only whitespace and the original closing line remain, and the next chunk is `openLine + "\n" + <whitespace> + <original closer>`.

# Change

When a fence split leaves only whitespace before the original closing line, the chunker closes the fence in the current chunk and drops that line, instead of reopening. Other splits are unchanged.

- The fast-close chunk is built exactly like the regular split's chunk: cut at `min(breakIdx, closer.start)`, then add `\n` only if the cut doesn't already end in one, then the synthetic closer. It is therefore never longer than the chunk the regular path would emit at that cut. If it would still exceed `limit`, the regular path runs instead.
- `findClosingFenceLine` only matches the line that actually closed the span: same marker character, marker length at least the opener's, and only spaces or tabs after it, matching `parseFenceSpans`. A fence that runs to EOF has no closing line and keeps the old path.
- `\r` is stripped before matching, so CRLF closers take the same path once #34057 lands.

Revision 2 (`131759a7`) fixes the `limit + 1` overflow reported by @mtcow and @ss251 at `a6a4b96`. When the body ended in a blank line, the first version kept both newlines.

  | Revision | Over-limit chunks | Empty or whitespace-only blocks | Highest limit with an empty block | Unclosed fences | Lost content |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | `develop` | 0 | 2,579 | 95 | 282 | 0 |

<details><summary>Wide fuzz script (run from the repository root with Bun)</summary>

```ts
import { chunkMarkdownText } from "../../packages/core/src/markdown/chunk.ts";

function analyze(chunk: string) {
  let open: { char: string; len: number; lines: string[] } | undefined;
  let empty = 0;
  for (const line of chunk.split("\n")) {
    const m = line.match(/^ {0,3}(`{3,}|~{3,})(.*)$/);
    if (!m) {
      open?.lines.push(line);
      continue;
    }
    if (!open) open = { char: m[1][0], len: m[1].length, lines: [] };
    else if (m[1][0] === open.char && m[1].length >= open.len && /^[ \t]*$/.test(m[2])) {
      if (open.lines.join("").trim() === "") empty++;
      open = undefined;
    } else open.lines.push(line);
  }
  return { unclosed: open ? 1 : 0, empty };
}

const strip = (value: string) =>
  value
    .split("\n")
    .filter((line) => !/^ {0,3}(`{3,}|~{3,})/.test(line))
    .join("")
    .replace(/\s+/g, "");

const fences: Array<[string, string]> = [
  ["```", "```"],
  ["```ts", "``` "],
  ["  ```", "  ```"],
  ["~~~", "~~~  "],
  ["````md", "`````"],
  ["```", "```\t"],
];
const gaps = ["\n", "\n\n", "\n \n", "\n\n\n"];
const totals = { cases: 0, unclosed: 0, empty: 0, overLimit: 0, lostContent: 0, inputEmpty: 0 };
for (const [opener, closer] of fences)
  for (const gap of gaps)
    for (const lines of [1, 2, 5])
      for (const width of [3, 9, 17]) {
        const body = Array.from({ length: lines }, (_, i) => `r${i}=${"ab".repeat(1 + ((i * width) % 9))}`);
        const block = `${opener}\n${body.join("\n")}${gap}${closer}`;
        const text = `Lead paragraph text.\n\n${block}\n\nMiddle words here.\n\n${block}\n\nTail.`;
        for (let limit = 12; limit <= 140; limit++) {
          totals.cases++;
          const chunks = chunkMarkdownText(text, limit);
          if (strip(chunks.join("\n")) !== strip(text)) totals.lostContent++;
          for (const chunk of chunks) {
            const r = analyze(chunk);
            totals.unclosed += r.unclosed;
            totals.empty += r.empty;
            if (r.empty) { (globalThis as any).maxEmptyLimit = Math.max((globalThis as any).maxEmptyLimit ?? 0, limit); }
            if (r.unclosed) { (globalThis as any).maxUnclosedLimit = Math.max((globalThis as any).maxUnclosedLimit ?? 0, limit); }
            if (chunk.length > limit) totals.overLimit++;
          }
        }
      }
console.log(JSON.stringify(totals)); console.log("maxUnclosedLimit", (globalThis as any).maxUnclosedLimit); console.log("maxEmptyLimit", (globalThis as any).maxEmptyLimit);
```
</details>

5a:Ta1e,# Relates to

Closes #34125

## Problem

`MESSAGE list_inbox` serves the cached `MessageRefStore` whenever it is non-empty and triggers a live `triage()` pull only when the cache is completely empty. The store keeps every ref a sweep ever handed it and never prunes read rows, so once the cached view contains only read messages, the action answers "No unread messages across connected platforms." for the rest of the process lifetime while the authoritative connectors hold new unread mail. That is a fabricated healthy-empty on a shipped product path: the empty claim was never checked against any adapter.

## The change (`plugins/plugin-assistant/src/features/messaging/triage/actions/listInbox.ts`)

- After the cached view is filtered to unread, a zero-unread result now falls through to the same live `triage()` pull an empty cache gets (same sources/sinceMs/limit), and the unread filter re-applies to the live result.
- The guard requires `messages.length > 0`, so an empty-cache start — which already pulled — never sweeps twice.
- The explicit empty-inbox result is unchanged; it is now only reported after the live pull confirms it.
- Header contract sentence updated to describe the fall-through.

## Validation (real execution, isolated worktree at this branch)

- **Red:** both new tests fail on develop `229502c357` exactly as the defect predicts — the adapter pull never runs (`seenOptions` length 0) and the all-read cache claims `total: 0` / "No unread messages…".
- **Green:** `bunx vitest run --config ./vitest.config.ts src/features/messaging/triage/__tests__/list-inbox-action.test.ts` → 9/9, including the new cases:
  - an all-read cache triggers exactly one live pull and the returned view comes from the live result;
  - "no unread" past an all-read cache is live-verified (the adapter pull is asserted observable);
  - pre-existing cached-unread, empty-cache, ranking, and error-boundary cases are unchanged.
- Full triage suite: 176/177 pass; the single failure (`send-consent.persistence.test.ts`) is a pre-existing pinned-runtime gate on this machine (node v22.23.2 vs pinned 24.15.0 in `sqlite-driver.ts`), byte-identical on clean `origin/develop` with the change stashed, and touches no file in this PR.
- `biome check` clean on both touched files.

# Relates to

`findEntityByName` (`plugins/plugin-assistant/src/entities.ts`) resolves a referent the model has to interpret, such as "him", "her", or a partial name, against the room. The `MESSAGE` send routing and the `CONTACT` action reach it whenever the referent is not a unique exact hit.

The resolution prompt's instructions say to "Consider context from recent messages for pronouns and references". On develop (base `65621da3a42`), that context is read with `getMemories({ tableName: "messages", roomId })` with no order, so adapters return it newest-first, and `formatRecentMessagesForResolution` renders it unsorted. The model therefore saw the latest mention first.

Example: "Dave sent the draft", then "Bob reviewed the draft", then "send him the doc". The prompt listed Bob's line above Dave's, so Dave read as the most recent antecedent and could be chosen.

This is the same defect fixed for the CHARACTER action in #33873 and for `recentConversationTexts` in #34060.

# Change

- The resolver reads the room `orderBy: "createdAt", orderDirection: "asc"`.
- `getRecentInteractions`, which takes the same array, only counts direct replies, so its scores are unchanged.

# Testing

- [x] Ran `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation (inline comment).

New `src/entities.resolution-transcript.test.ts` runs on a real in-memory SQLite runtime, with only `TEXT_SMALL` stubbed to capture the prompt:

- Setup: a group room with Owner, Bob, and Dave, plus two stored turns.
- Action: `findEntityByName` runs for "him".
- Assertion: the prompt lists "Dave sent the draft" before "Bob reviewed the draft".

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
5d:T1426,# Relates to

Queue-cleared audit finding on the shipped inbox path (no open mission-ready issue covers it; related inbox window fixes #33922/#33926 already merged for chat/X, Gmail was left behind).

`fetchGmailMessages` in `plugins/plugin-inbox/src/inbox/message-fetcher.ts` capped the triage read to `maxResults: limit` and sliced to `limit` *before* applying `sinceIso`. Triage orders by `triage_score DESC, received_at DESC` (not recency), so a high-triage out-of-window row fills the capped window and valid in-window messages are dropped. With `limit: 1` + `sinceIso`, the fetch can return `[]` while a valid row exists.

# Change

- `fetchGmailMessages`: compute `sinceMs` before the triage read; when `sinceIso` is present, fetch uncapped (no `maxResults`), filter by `sinceIso` first over the full feed, then slice to `limit`. No-`since` path keeps the existing capped read.
- Added `plugins/plugin-inbox/src/inbox/message-fetcher.gmail-since.test.ts` (3 deterministic stub tests, no network/DB): in-window row past a capped out-of-window triage row is returned; triage read is uncapped when `sinceIso` is present and still capped when absent.

Queue-cleared audit finding on the shipped LifeOps inbox path (bug tier). No open `mission-ready` issue covers it. Gmail had the identical sinceIso-before-limit defect and was repaired in #34017 (merged); X DMs were left behind with the same capped-read-then-filter order.

**Reproduced failure:** `fetchXDmMessages` (`plugins/plugin-inbox/src/inbox/message-fetcher.ts`) capped the cache read to `limit` (`getXDms({ limit, inbound: true })`) and only then dropped rows older than `sinceIso`. With `limit: 1` + `sinceIso`, a single out-of-window DM fills the capped window and a valid in-window DM is lost: pre-fix the new regression returns `[]` where `['new-valid']` is expected, and the read is issued with `{ limit: 1, inbound: true }` instead of uncapped.

# Change

- `fetchXDmMessages`: compute `sinceMs` before the cache read; when `sinceIso` is present, fetch uncapped (no `limit`), filter by `sinceIso` first over the full inbound set, then slice to `limit`. No-`since` path keeps the existing capped read. Mirrors the merged Gmail repair exactly.
- Added `plugins/plugin-inbox/src/inbox/message-fetcher.xdm-since.test.ts` (3 deterministic stub tests, no network/DB): in-window DM past a capped out-of-window row is returned; the cache read is uncapped when `sinceIso` is present and still capped when absent.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Applies `sinceIso` before the result `limit` in the X DM inbox source, so time-filtered inbox reads no longer lose valid in-window DMs behind out-of-window rows that filled the capped window.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

5f:Tdab,Fixes #34109

# Relates to

This is the follow-up to #34103, which covers goal review writes. Check-in replies had the same stale full-row write.

`GoalsCheckinService.recordCheckinResponse` (`plugins/plugin-goals/src/services/checkin.ts`) is reached from the `OWNER_GOALS` checkin subaction and the check-in reply flow. On develop (base `65621da3a42`) it:

1. reads the goal;
2. completes the fired check-in task through the scheduling runner (async);
3. writes the whole goal back from step 1 with `updateGoal`, which rewrites title, status, description, cadence, and all metadata.

An edit made during step 2, such as a title or status change, or a goal review recording `computedGoalReview`, was reverted. For example, renaming "Run a 5k" to "Run a 10k" while a reply was being recorded left the goal titled "Run a 5k".

# Change

- After completing the task, the goal is re-read, and the new log entry is appended to its current `checkinLog`.
- New `GoalsRepository.updateGoalCheckin` sets only `review_state` and `updated_at`, and merges `metadata.checkinLog` into the stored metadata in SQL (`COALESCE(metadata_json, '{}')::jsonb || {checkinLog}`). plugin-goals is PostgreSQL/PGlite-only.
- A goal deleted while the task completed now fails 404, instead of reporting success for a write that updated nothing.
- The audit event and the return value are unchanged.
- The new repository method sits at a different spot in `goals-repository.ts` than #34103's `updateGoalReview`, so the two PRs merge independently.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (base `65621da3a42`).
- [x] Ran `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation (JSDoc and inline comment).

New case in `src/services/checkin.real.test.ts`, which uses a real runtime with PGlite, the real scheduling spine, and the deterministic model provider. The first `getGoal` read returns its snapshot and then applies a concurrent title edit. After the response is recorded, the goal keeps "Run a 10k", has `reviewState: on_track`, and has the new log entry.

Results:
- Against develop's `checkin.ts`, the test fails (`expected 'Run a 5k' to be 'Run a 10k'`).
- `checkin.real.test.ts` passes 5/5 (real-runtime config), and plugin-goals passes 10 files / 100 tests.
- `bun run verify` passes on this head: 298/298 Turbo typecheck/lint tasks plus every audit, exit 0.

# Relates to

Queue-cleared develop-workflow-health finding (no open issue carries `mission-ready` at selection time; verified against the live issue list and the open-PR queue immediately before starting). The required `CI` workflow's **Zero-Key scenario runner** job has been failing on the develop lineage:

- run [37390002351](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/37390002351) @ `d2aa02410c` — `Zero-Key scenario runner` / `All Tests Passed` failed
- run [37370973597](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/37370973597) @ `84b0efe0d6` — `Zero-Key scenario runner` failed

In both, exactly one scenario fails while the other 26 pass:

```
| deterministic-github-actions-routes | failed | 10926ms | assertResponse: expected status 200, saw 404 |
Totals: 26 passed, 1 failed, 0 skipped of 27
```

**Root cause:** plugin-github consolidated its credential routes onto the canonical return-shape `routeHandler` contract (`packages/host/src/api/http-plugin.ts`). The real host dispatcher (`packages/agent/src/api/dispatch-route.ts` line ~564) serves **both** handler shapes (`route.handler || route.routeHandler`), but the scenario-runner's mini HTTP server (`packages/testing/scenario-runner/src/executor.ts`, `startScenarioApiServer`) dispatched only the legacy Express-shaped `handler`. The scenario's final API turn `GET /api/github/token` therefore no longer matched any route and 404ed — a harness regression, not a product regression: the product serves the route correctly through `dispatchRoute`/the Hono adapter.

# Change

`packages/testing/scenario-runner/src/executor.ts` only:

- `startScenarioApiServer` now dispatches routes that define the canonical `routeHandler`, building a faithful `RouteHandlerContext` (`signal` aborted on transport close, parsed `body`/`rawBody`, `params`, `query`, normalized lowercase `headers`, `method`, `path`, `runtime`, `inProcess: false`, `isTrustedLocal: true` on the loopback harness) exactly mirroring `dispatchRoute`.
- `writeRouteHandlerResult` serializes the `RouteHandlerResult` with the host adapter's defaults from `packages/agent/src/api/hono-adapter.ts`: explicit result headers win; content type defaults from the body kind (string → text/plain, bytes → octet-stream, else JSON); null-body statuses end without a body. A `stream` result fails fast with an explicit 500 error instead of a silently empty success response.
- The legacy `handler` dispatch path is byte-equivalent (only restructured into a narrowed local), so routes that have not migrated are unaffected.

No product code, route table, plugin, or scenario definition changes. Material risk is limited to scenario api-turn dispatch inside the test harness.
