61:Tc60,Fixes #34034

# Relates to

`GoogleGmailAdapter` (`plugins/plugin-google-workspace/src/lifeops-message-adapter.ts`) declares `worlds: "multi"`, and `MESSAGE` exposes `worldIds` as "Account/server scopes for inbox ops". On develop (base `c58336b70c0`), however, both `listMessagesImpl` and `searchMessagesImpl` resolved `const accountId = opts.worldIds?.[0] ?? DEFAULT_GOOGLE_ACCOUNT_ID` and queried only that account.

So `MESSAGE triage` / `list_inbox` / `search_inbox` with `worldIds: ["work", "personal"]` fetched only "work". `cacheAndFilter` kept both worlds, but "personal" was never read, so its mail was silently missing.

Sibling comparison:
- Discord's `resolveChannelIds` treats `worldIds` as a set and covers every server.
- The same file's `searchGmailChannels` already loops over every requested label and merges the newest-first union.

# Change

- Both paths query every distinct requested account, or the default account when none is given, using the existing per-account query.
- Each ref is mapped with its own account id.
- Results are merged newest-first (ties keep provider order) before `cacheAndFilter` applies the shared limit.

A single-account request behaves as before.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (base `c58336b70c0`).
- [x] Ran `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation (JSDoc).

New `lifeops-message-adapter.test.ts` case "lists and searches every requested Gmail account, newest first":
- The stub Google service is keyed by `accountId`, with two "work" messages and one "personal" message.
- `listMessages({ worldIds: ["work","personal"], limit: 2 })` returns `work_new`, `personal_mid`.
- `searchMessages` returns all three, newest first.
- Both accounts are queried for both operations.

Results:
- Against develop's adapter, the test fails: it returns `work_new`, `work_old`, so "personal" is never read.
- The plugin-google-workspace suite passes 45 files / 494 tests.
- `bun run verify` passes on this head: 298/298 Turbo typecheck/lint tasks plus every audit, exit 0.

62:Ted4,Fixes #34061

# Relates to

`recentConversationTexts` (`packages/core/src/actions/recent-context.ts`) reads the room's `messages` with no order. The adapters default to newest-first (plugin-sql orders `createdAt desc, id desc`). The texts are returned in that order, followed by the provider-state texts.

Callers render the result as the conversation:
- the grounded action reply's `Complete conversation: …` (`plugins/plugin-assistant/src/actions/grounded-action-reply.ts`);
- the LifeOps task, goal, and deferred-draft extractors (`extract-task-plan.ts`, `extract-goal-plan.ts`, `lifeops-deferred-draft.ts`);
- the calendar, health, entity, book-travel, app-block, and scheduling actions.

So on develop (base `65621da3a42`) every one of these prompts showed the stored history backwards. If the owner says "remind me at 4pm" and then "actually make it 5pm", the extractor saw the correction first and the instruction it corrects last.

This is the same defect fixed for the CHARACTER action in #33873, one level down in the shared helper.

# Change

- The room read passes `orderBy: "createdAt", orderDirection: "asc"`.
- This read is unbounded, so the runtime's coalesced newest-first scan (eligible only for bounded windows) was never involved. Nothing else changes.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (base `65621da3a42`).
- [x] Ran `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation (inline comment).

New case in `plugins/plugin-assistant/src/actions/recent-conversation-texts.test.ts`: a real in-memory SQLite runtime stores "remind me at 4pm", then "actually make it 5pm", and `recentConversationTexts` returns them in that order. The existing cases used order-agnostic mocks. The same test then stores 120 more turns and gets all 122 back in order. That shows no default read window: `runtime.getMemories` forwards `limit ?? count` (undefined here), plugin-sql adds `LIMIT` only when `effectiveLimit !== undefined`, and plugin-sqlite slices only when a limit is given. So ascending order cannot select an oldest page instead of the newest one.

Results:
- Against develop's `recent-context.ts`, the test fails (`expected ['actually make it 5pm', …] to deeply equal ['remind me at 4pm', …]`).
- Callers' suites pass under pinned Node 24.15.0:

| Suite | Result |
| --- | --- |
| plugin-calendar | 1230 passed |
| plugin-assistant `src/actions` + `src/runtime` | 693 passed |
| plugin-personal-assistant tests using these helpers | 398 passed |

- `bun run verify` passes on this head: 298/298 Turbo typecheck/lint tasks plus every audit, exit 0.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
63:T162b,# Relates to

`clawbackForReversal` (`packages/cloud/api/src/queue/stripe-event.ts`) handles `charge.refunded` and `charge.dispute.funds_withdrawn`. It only claws back credits in proportion to the reversed share of the charge when a Stripe Checkout order exists for the payment intent. Every other grant is clawed back 1:1 in dollars.

Durable auto top-ups never create a checkout order, but an affiliate-attributed auto top-up charges more than it credits: the credited base plus the affiliate markup plus the 20% platform fee (`buildRequestSnapshot` in `auto-top-up.ts`). A partial refund or dispute on such a charge therefore removed more credits than the reversed share of the grant.

Example: a $10 credit with a 30% markup charges $15.00. A $7.50 (half) refund or dispute clawed back $7.50 of the 10 credits instead of 5. Full reversals were already capped at the grant, so only partial reversals were affected.

# Change

- When there is no checkout order, read the fee-inclusive total from the grant's auto top-up metadata. The settlement copies the signed PaymentIntent metadata (`base_amount`, `total_charged`, fee lines) onto the credit transaction. The metadata is parsed with the existing `autoTopUpChargeBreakdownFromMetadata`, which rejects rows whose lines do not add up.
- The checkout-order proportional formula is then applied to that total.
- The clawback row records `auto_top_up_attempt_id`, `original_charge_usd` and `original_credits_granted`, as the checkout branch already does.
- Legacy 1:1 top-ups, which carry no fee metadata, are unchanged.

# Testing

- `packages/cloud/api` `bun test __tests__/stripe-dispute-reinstatement.pglite.test.ts`: **10/10**. This drives the real queue consumer and the real credits service against PGlite. 64:T5bc,Fixes #34178

### Problem
`parsePdfSpecDate` only clamps each field. February 30 / April 31 / non-leap February 29 still reach `Date` and overflow into March/May, so `getDocumentInfo` reports a wrong `CreationDate`/`ModDate` instead of omitting the bad value.

### Fix
After building the declared wall-clock (local or UTC), require the `Date` getters to round-trip year/month/day/hour/minute/second. Impossible combinations return `undefined`. UT offsets are applied only after that check, via millisecond arithmetic. Existing clamp defaults for completely out-of-range components (month 13 → January, day 40 → 1) are unchanged.

### Tests
Three new cases in `pdf-spec-date.test.ts`:
- February 30 dropped for `Z`, offset, and local (no UT) forms
- April 31 dropped
- non-leap February 29 dropped; leap `D:20240229…Z` still parses

Before (develop parser + new tests): 3 failed | 7 passed in `pdf-spec-date.test.ts`. After: 10/10.

<img width="800" height="201" alt="after" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/0858ef7c-44f6-4930-a37a-3e4796c77d77" />

66:Teac,# Problem and change

The contribution policy was missing from CONTRIBUTING.md. The repository also had 183 nested AGENTS.md files, and the README told contributors to read those local guides.

The full policy now starts CONTRIBUTING.md. README.md and the root AGENTS.md highlight its required rules. Issue and PR templates link to CONTRIBUTING.md. All 183 nested AGENTS.md files are removed. The root guides say not to add them again. Links to removed guides are repaired.

The policy requires approved MVP scope, human discussion and approval before new features, proof of useful improvement, simpler code and reuse, real end-to-end results, UI video evidence and test steps, and ASD-STE100 writing. It states that maintainers will close unnecessary work and apply contributor penalties.

This is the repository owner's requested guide cleanup. No product feature or MVP requirement changes.

# Why this approach

I checked the root guides, tracked agent-guide files, links, and references to AGENTS.md. One full contribution policy and short notices at the entry points reduce duplication. Removing the nested files follows the owner's explicit instruction. Keeping copies or adding a new enforcement script would work against that request.

This is a mechanical documentation change across packages. Product support for AGENTS.md in user workspaces is unchanged. No package behavior is changed.

# Validation

- The tracked file list contains exactly one AGENTS.md, at the root. Before this change, it contained 184.
- All 29 local Markdown links and section links in changed documents resolve.
- No Markdown link in a remaining tracked document points to a deleted guide.
- All issue template YAML and the issue chooser parse successfully.
- The existing PR evidence checker passes its 14 built-in cases with Node 24.15.0.
- `git diff --check` passes.

No new tests were added. Full repository verification was not rerun for this documentation-only change. The earlier attempt in this worktree stopped because the `yaml` dependency was not installed; it is not recorded as passing.

# How to review

1. Read the important notice and policy in CONTRIBUTING.md.
2. Read README.md and the root AGENTS.md. Check that each highlights the same requirements and states that only the root agent guide is kept.
3. Run `git ls-files '*AGENTS.md'`. Expect only `AGENTS.md`.
4. Inspect the deleted files and repaired links. Check that the change does not edit product behavior.
5. Run `node packages/scripts/check-pr-evidence.ts --self-test`. Expect all 14 cases to pass.

68:Td1e,Fixes #34102

# Relates to

`GET /api/approvals` (`packages/agent/src/api/approval-routes.ts`) backs the app's pending-actions panel (`ElizaClient` → `/api/approvals`, default `limit=50`, `state=pending`). On develop (base `65621da3a42`) it:

1. calls `queue.list({ ..., limit })`, which `PgApprovalQueue.list` runs as `WHERE agent_id … AND state = 'pending' ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 50`;
2. only then filters out `device_action` requests in JS. Those are served solely by `/api/client-devices` with the enrollment credential.

The device rows therefore consume the limit:
- If the 50 newest pending rows are device actions and an older `send_email` approval is pending, the response is `approvals: []` and the email approval never appears.
- With 45 device rows and 10 normal ones, 5 normal approvals disappear.

The response has no truncation flag, so the panel reads as complete.

# Change

- `ApprovalListFilter` gains an optional `excludeAction`, added in both the assistant type and the LifeOps mirror. `PgApprovalQueue.list` applies it as `action <> …` in SQL, before `LIMIT`.
- The order gains an `id DESC` tie-break, so equal `created_at` rows cannot trade places.
- The route passes `excludeAction: "device_action"`. Its existing JS `device_action` filter stays as defence in depth, so the device exclusion is never looser than before.
- The LifeOps queue delegates `list` unchanged.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (base `65621da3a42`).
- [x] Ran `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation (JSDoc and inline comment).

New `approval-list-exclude-action.integration.test.ts` uses the real approval queue on PGlite with migrations:
- It enqueues one older `send_email` approval and three newer requests rewritten to `device_action`.
- `list({ state: "pending", excludeAction: "device_action", limit: 2 })` returns the email approval.

Results:
- Against develop's store, the test fails: the two newest device rows are returned.
- The approval service suite passes 2 files / 24 tests.
- `bun run verify` passes on this head: 298/298 Turbo typecheck/lint tasks plus every audit, exit 0.

6c:Tc72,Fixes #34072

# Relates to

When the owner approves a `send_email` through `RESOLVE_REQUEST`, `persistSentMailCommitments` (`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/actions/resolve-request.ts`) projects the email's commitments ("I'll send the deck tomorrow") into the commitment ledger.

On develop (base `65621da3a42`) it calls `extractCommitmentLedgerRecords` without a `timeZone`, so the extractor resolves deadlines with `input.timeZone ?? "UTC"` (`lifeops/commitments/ledger.ts`). As a result, relative and explicit deadlines land on UTC days at 17:00 UTC. Example for an owner in America/Los_Angeles:

| Email text (sent 2026-10-06 18:30 PDT) | Due on develop | Expected |
| --- | --- | --- |
| "I'll send the deck tomorrow" | Oct 8 10:00 PDT | Oct 7 17:00 PDT |
| An explicit "2026-07-10" | 10:00 PDT | 17:00 PDT |

#32886 fixed the meeting-transcript path, which resolves `resolveOwnerTimeZone(runtime, transcriptInstant)` (`commitments/transcript-hook.ts`). The sent-mail path was left on UTC.

# Change

- `persistSentMailCommitments` resolves `resolveOwnerTimeZone(runtime, sentAt)` and passes it to the extractor, the same pattern as the transcript hook.
- With no owner fact, that helper falls back to the configured `TIMEZONE`, as everywhere else.

# Testing

- [x] Ran `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation (inline comment).

New case in `test/resolve-request-executor.test.ts`, "resolves a sent-mail commitment deadline on the owner's civil day":
- The runtime is configured for America/Los_Angeles, and the approved email says "by 2026-07-10".
- The ledger record's `dueAt` is `2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z` (17:00 PDT).

Results:
- Against develop, the test fails (`2026-07-10T17:00:00.000Z`).

6f:T23ae,# Relates to

Restores the red `canonical / Plugin Tests (3/4)` lane on `develop` head `65621da3a4` (Develop Full run [37395854516](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/37395854516)). No open issue tracks this; the failure is reproduced below on that exact head before any branch was created.

**Reproduced failure** (`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/test/connector-payload-fuzz.test.ts > scheduled-task dispatch path fuzz > resolves arbitrary garbage channel targets to typed outcomes, never a throw`):

```
Caused by: AssertionError: DispatchResult must be an object (never a bare boolean): expected 'undefined' to be 'object'
❯ expectTypedDispatchResult test/connector-payload-fuzz.test.ts:72:5
Counterexample: ["x"]  seed: -2125920663  path: "5"
```

For a garbage channel target like `"x"`, `pickChannelKey` derives the channel key `x` — which **is a registered channel** (X) — so dispatch proceeds into owner/contact target resolution. `resolveScheduledTaskChannelTarget` → `resolveOwnerChannelTarget` → `resolveOwnerEntityId` throws `OWNER_ENTITY_LOOKUP_FAILED` when room/world storage reads fail, and that call sat **outside every typed boundary**: the throw passed the production dispatcher, and the runner records thrown dispatch errors with only `metadata.lastDispatchError` — `metadata.lastDispatchResult` stays unset, so the stored outcome leaves the connectors-return-typed-`DispatchResult` contract.

Isolated deterministic repro (harness with the production dispatcher, target `"x"`, on `65621da3a4`): fire result `status: "failed"`, `lastDispatchResult: undefined`, log transitions `fired → failed`.

# Change

- `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/scheduled-task/runtime-wiring.ts`: wrap the single `resolveScheduledTaskChannelTarget` call in the dispatcher's send path. A resolution failure is now reported through `runtime.reportError` (`lifeops:scheduled-task:dispatch-target-resolve`) and returned as a **typed retryable** `DispatchResult` (`transport_error`, `userActionable: false`, `retryAfterMinutes: 5` via `applyDispatchPolicy`), so the policy parks the task for a same-step retry instead of stranding it as a result-less failure. The neighboring `!target` branch (typed `disconnected`) is unchanged.
- New `TARGET_RESOLUTION_RETRY_MINUTES = 5`, matching the established render-failure backoff so both pre-send preparation failures retry on the same schedule.
- `test/connector-payload-fuzz.test.ts`: pin the recorded CI counterexample with a deterministic case asserting the stored `lastDispatchResult` is a typed `{ ok: false, reason: "transport_error" }` with positive backoff, the transition is `dispatch_retried`/`escalated`, and the row is parked at a concrete next attempt.

Material risks: none known — the throw path previously produced no typed result at all, so every consumer of `lastDispatchResult` already handles `ok: false` results; the retry path is the runner's enforced policy path (`decideDispatchPolicy` retry-on-backoff).

# Testing

Red-then-green on the real harness (production dispatcher, real runner, in-memory stores):

- Base `65621da3a4` + new test only: `AssertionError: expected 'failed' to be 'scheduled'` — reproduces the CI counterexample deterministically.

72:Tea0,Fixes #34040

# Relates to

The agent's own entity is created once in `AgentRuntime.initialize` (`packages/core/src/runtime.ts`) with `names: [character.name]`, and only when no entity exists yet. Nothing updates `names` afterwards. Every rename path (`PUT /api/character`, the `CHARACTER` action through `CharacterPersistenceService`, and first-run setup) mutates `runtime.character.name` and persists through `runtime.updateAgent(agentId, { name, … })`, which updates only the agent row.

Prompts label speakers with `entity.names[0]`, and the agent has no special case:
- core `formatMessages` (`packages/core/src/utils.ts`);
- `RECENT_MESSAGES` (`plugins/plugin-assistant/.../recentMessages.ts`);
- `getEntityDetails` (`plugins/plugin-assistant/src/entities.ts`).

So after renaming "Eliza" to "Nova", every later prompt still showed the agent's own turns as `Eliza: …` and listed `Eliza` in the room roster, while the system prompt and bio said Nova. A restart did not repair it, because creation is skipped once the entity exists.

# Change

- `AgentRuntime.updateAgent`: after a successful update of this agent with a non-empty `name`, move that name to the front of the agent entity's `names`. Older names are kept as aliases. All three rename paths already call this, so none of them changes.
- `initialize`: when the agent entity already exists and `names[0]` differs from `character.name`, apply the same sync. This repairs installs that were renamed before this fix.
- One private helper does both, and it is a no-op when the name already matches.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (base `65621da3a42`).
- [x] Ran `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation (JSDoc and inline comment).

| A second runtime on the same database, character renamed to Nova | `initialize` repairs `names[0]` to `"Nova"`. |

73:T5c6,Fixes #34186

## Problem

`cleanUrl` (`plugins/plugin-discord/utils.ts`), used by `extractUrls` for inbound URL enrichment, removed every trailing `)`. URLs whose path ends in a balanced parenthesis, such as `https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercury_(planet)`, were cut to `.../Mercury_(planet`. That broken URL is what the message manager then fetched and attached.

## Fix

Take `)` out of the trailing-punctuation character class. Inside the same loop, strip a trailing `)` only when the URL has more `)` than `(`. Wrapping parentheses, markdown link closers and other trailing punctuation are handled as before (`(see https://example.com/a).` → `https://example.com/a`).

## Tests

New `plugins/plugin-discord/__tests__/extract-urls.test.ts` (5 cases). 4 fail on develop and all 5 pass with the fix.

76:Td90,Fixes #34088

# Relates to

On develop (base `65621da3a42`), the SQLite adapter's `updateMemories` and `updateRelationships` (`plugins/plugin-sqlite/records.ts`) merge provided metadata into the stored object (`{ ...existing.metadata, ...memory.metadata }`). plugin-sql replaces it: `updateMemory` writes the provided `metadata` jsonb, and `updateRelationship` writes `relationship.metadata`. The core `updateMemories` contract says nothing about merging.

As a result, on SQLite no caller can remove a metadata key. The production case is reply recovery in `packages/agent/src/api/conversation-routes.ts`:
- `clearRecoveredReplyFailureMarkers` drops `chatFailureKind`, `failureKind`, `syntheticChatFailure` and `elizaSyntheticFailure` from a recovered reply's metadata, and writes the rest back through `runtime.updateMemory`.
- On PostgreSQL/PGlite the markers are gone.
- On SQLite they survive, so GET messages still projects `failureKind`, and the recovered reply keeps rendering as failed with a Retry button.

I checked the two production callers that pass metadata to `updateMemory`: the attachment transcript backfill and the fact-dedupe upgrade. Both already send the complete object (`{ ...existing.metadata, … }`), so replace semantics drop nothing for them.

# Change

- Provided metadata replaces the stored object.
- Omitted metadata keeps it, so content-only or embedding-only updates are unchanged.
- The same applies to relationships. `patchTaskMetadata`, which is a merge by name, is unchanged.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (base `65621da3a42`).
- [x] Ran `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation (inline comments).

The new `adapter.test.ts` case runs on a real SQLite file:

77:T1168,# Relates to

#34058 counts a JSON fence opener longer than three backticks, which is the right fix, but it drops the check that the value ends in a fence. `end = value.length - fenceLength` then slices off that many characters whatever they are. A reply that only starts with a fence is parsed with its tail cut off. `unwrapWholeCodeFence` feeds `parseJsonObject` (`packages/core/src/runtime/json-output.ts`) and the JSON action output (`packages/core/src/actions/json-model-output.ts`).

On #34058's head `71fbc3b6`:

| input | result |
|---|---|
| ```` ```json\n{"a":1}\nnot a fence ```` | `{"a":1}\nnot a fe` |
| ```` ```json\n{"a":1}\n``` trailing ```` | `{"a":1}\n``` trail` |
| ```` ```\n{"a":12345} ```` | `{"a":12` |

`parseJsonObject` uses that string and does not fall back to the original, so `parseJsonObject('```\n{"a":12345}')` returns null instead of `{ a: 12345 }`.

# Change

`unwrapWholeCodeFence` (`packages/core/src/markdown/code.ts`) takes the opener length (three or more backticks) and returns null unless the value ends in a run of the same length. A longer opener unwraps. A missing or shorter closer does not.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop`.
- [x] Ran `bun install` and `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation. (N/A: internal parser behavior.)

Results:

- New tests in `packages/core/test/unwrap-whole-code-fence.test.ts`: a 3-backtick fence, 4- and 6-backtick fences with and without a `json` label, compact unlabeled fences, and four inputs that do not end in a matching closer. `parseJsonObject` reads a 4-backtick fence as `{ a: 1 }` and still reads `{ a: 12345 }` when the reply only starts with a fence.
- On `develop`: the two longer-fence tests fail (7 tests, 2 failed). On this branch: 7/7 pass.
- `packages/core`: `bun run typecheck` and `bun run lint:check` are clean.
- `bun run verify` (pinned Node 24.15.0 / Bun 1.4.2): exit 0 at `53fb2541`, full chain. The turbo step (298/298) ran at `--concurrency=2`, because the default 8-way run is killed (exit 137) on this 31 GB host.

78:T620,Fixes #34185

## Problem

`describeRecurrence` always rendered RRULE `UNTIL` with `timeZone: "UTC"`. Google stores end-of-local-day cutoffs as UTC date-times (e.g. `20110617T065959Z` for a Los Angeles series that ends Jun 16), so clarification replies like `"…" repeats weekly until …` showed the wrong calendar day.

<img width="1083" height="836" alt="before" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/dc9ecce1-f1ef-40ba-8b4f-9e9109ba9bf9" />

## Fix

- Optional `timeZone` on `describeRecurrence`; date-time UNTIL labels use a valid event IANA zone.
- Date-only `UNTIL=YYYYMMDD` still labels in UTC (the date is already civil).
- Pass `event.timezone` from calendar-handler recurrence clarification / update-context call sites.

## Tests

- `test/recurrence.test.ts`: date-time UNTIL in LA / NY / Tokyo; date-only UNTIL stable across zones.
- Focused file: **30 passed** (after). Before: **1 failed | 29 passed** on the new assertions.
- Related: `calendar-handler.recurrence-scope.test.ts` + `recurrence-guard.test.ts`: **19 passed**.
- `biome check` / `biome format` clean on touched files.
- Full package vitest had unrelated env timeouts/OOM (pglite/hooks); not regressions of this change. `tsc --noEmit` for the package was OOM-killed (exit 137) in this box — note env gap.

7c:Ta07,# Relates to

ERC-20 `name` and `symbol` are optional metadata methods; the standard says clients must not assume they exist ([EIP-20](https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-20)). `getTokenBalance` queried them in the same rejecting `Promise.all` as `balanceOf` and `decimals`, so a valid token with either optional method omitted made its balance unreadable.

# Change

- Keep the balance and decimals reads authoritative.
- Use `UNKNOWN` and `Unknown token` when optional symbol/name reads fail, allowing callers to still see the balance.
- Add a regression test where both optional calls revert and the balance/decimals calls succeed.

# Testing

- `git diff --check` — passed.
- `bun test plugins/plugin-wallet/src/sdk/tokens/transfers.test.ts` — not run: Bun is unavailable in this environment. The focused regression test is included for CI.
- Targets `develop` at `65621da3a424f8fcdce760db5c9c414117082e18`; changed source paths were unchanged from that base.
- Documentation update: N/A — this corrects runtime handling of optional standard metadata.
